I fixed one of the two warnings by editing the file. The other needs an extension install.

1. **Line 8, deprecated setting:** renamed `typescript.tsdk` to `js/ts.tsdk.path`. Older VS Code versions don't know the new name and will ignore it.
2. **Line 2, "Value is not accepted":** the setting is correct. The Biome extension just isn't installed in your VS Code, so `biomejs.biome` isn't in the list of allowed values. It's already recommended in `.vscode/extensions.json`. Install it with:
   ```bash
   code --install-extension biomejs.biome
   ```
   After that the warning goes away, and format-on-save and fix-on-save start working.

Not committed.

Read docs. Where things stand:

**Product.** Jarso is a pocket-money learning app for kids aged 11–14. Parent sets the allowance and confirms every handover; kid decides how to use it. Spend and Save jars, one savings goal, a money diary, daily missions. AI guide comes later. Money is recorded only, nothing real moves.

**Built so far.** Foundation scaffold only: auth (email sign-in), `profiles` table, CI, docs, ADRs. No domain modules yet.

**Planned next: first milestone.** One parent and one child complete the full loop:
- onboarding and linking
- allowance schedule, then due, then handover posts to the ledger
- purchase, with a limit notice to the parent
- savings withdrawal request, decision, handover
- in-app inbox

Modules: `family`, `allowance`, `ledger`, `jars`, `diary`, `requests`, `limits`, `notifications`.

**Already decided (ADRs 0004–0006).**
- Kids sign in with family code + nickname + PIN, no email.
- `families` hub supports many parents and many children.
- Notifications are derived records: inbox for everyone, email for parents.
- `ledger_entries` table is append-only and is the only source of truth for money.

**Still open.**
- Partial handover rule (architecture assumes it closes the occurrence and records a shortfall)
- Reminder timing
- Spending limit types
- Purchase bigger than Spend balance
- Which jar gets emergency money
- Schedule edge cases
- Parent verification

If you'd rather skip questions and have me start planning, say so.

What's driving next step: hackathon demo deadline where judges watch first-milestone flow end to end, or something else?

Here's what I have. Anything I've marked as a guess is my call, not something you said.

**Scope.** First milestone, demo-ready in 1–2 weeks. Built as five vertical slices, one PR each, each one demoable and tested:
1. Family and onboarding: child-started and parent-started; parent confirms email and ticks a guardian consent box, which is stored with a timestamp.
2. Ledger, allowance schedule, due occurrences and handover.
3. Purchases and limits.
4. Savings withdrawals.
5. Inbox and reminders.

**Decisions.**
- **Partial handover:** confirming closes the occurrence and records the shortfall.
- **Overspend:** off by default. Parent can turn on "allow Spend below zero". When it's off, a purchase bigger than Spend is blocked with a friendly explanation. This narrows FR14's "always recorded": limits still never block, only the balance can.
- **Limits:** per purchase, per week and per month, any mix. Each breach sends the parent a notice.
- **Reminders:** a notice the day the allowance falls due, then every 3 days until confirmed.
- **Missed occurrences:** each stays due on its own; no roll-up, no expiry.
- **Schedule changes:** apply from the next occurrence. Parent picks the start date.
- **Docs:** I'll update REQUIREMENTS.md "Open decisions" and the architecture doc.

**My guesses (correct me):**
- Week = Monday–Sunday, month = calendar month, both in the family's timezone.
- Email to parents for new requests and limit breaches ships in slice 5 with the inbox (ADR 0006), using Resend.
- Deposits and emergency funds stay out of this milestone, as planned.
- Demo runs on the one hosted Supabase project, with a seed script for one demo family.
- UI follows docs/design.md, mobile-first.

Next I'd write a design spec at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-28-first-milestone-design.md`. A reviewer reads it before you do, and I may come back once with a few questions.

What's wrong or missing?

The design spec for the first milestone is ready for you to read: [docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-28-first-milestone-design.md](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-28-first-milestone-design.md). It is not committed yet, and we're on `main`.

A reviewer read it as if they had to build from it and raised six questions. You answered four, and these changes went in:
- **Deposits** are now in slice 4. They are a first-milestone acceptance criterion in REQUIREMENTS.md, so leaving them out was my mistake.
- **Demo clock:** each family gets a time offset that its parent can move forward, so judges can watch allowances fall due and reminders fire live. It is off unless both an environment flag and a per-family flag are set. Every time-based function takes an injectable "now" from slice 2 onward.
- **Several children per parent:** parent screens are scoped by child, with a child switcher.
- **Invites work without email:** each invitation gives a copyable link and a short code. Email is sent as well once Resend is configured.

Calls I made that you should check while reading:
1. **Child-started onboarding:** the child gets no account until the parent accepts. Until then the child sees a "show this to your parent" screen with the link and code. The form is rate-limited.
2. **Existing family:** if the accepting parent already has a family, the child joins it.
3. **Currency:** one per family, picked when the family is created. Currencies with no decimal places, like JPY, are supported.
4. **Time zone:** each family has a time zone, set from the browser. Limit weeks run Monday to Sunday and months are calendar months, both in that time zone.
5. **Due dates on the 29th–31st** move to the last day of shorter months.
6. **Deposits** never take Spend below zero, even when the parent allows overspending.
7. **Demo parents** are created by the seed script, because Supabase's built-in sign-up email is heavily rate-limited.

Once you approve, I'll create a branch, commit the spec and write the slice-by-slice plan. What needs changing?

The plan for slice 1 (family and onboarding) is written and committed on `docs/first-milestone-spec`: [docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-28-slice-1-family-onboarding.md](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-28-slice-1-family-onboarding.md).

**Why only slice 1.** The spec covers seven slices, too many for one plan. Every later slice builds on the schema and helpers slice 1 creates, so I'll write each next plan after the previous slice merges.

**What's in it:**
- **Task 0** puts the decisions we made into REQUIREMENTS.md, the architecture doc and the domain doc. It runs on this branch, so the docs PR carries the spec, the plan and those updates together.
- **Tasks 1–4** are the database work:
  - a test setup for database rules (Supabase's built-in SQL test runner), added to CI
  - the family tables and access rules
  - child accounts with PIN lockout and rate limits
  - invitations from children
- **Tasks 5–6** are the server layer: codes, input validation, error mapping, the services, role checks, and creating child logins and sessions. Task 6 starts with a throwaway test of child session creation, the riskiest piece. It also checks whether Supabase accepts the made-up `.invalid` email domain for child accounts.
- **Tasks 7–9** are the screens: parent setup and family page, the child-started onboarding page plus the parent's page for accepting an invitation, and child sign-in.
- **Task 10** adds end-to-end tests for both onboarding paths, family isolation and PIN lockout, then the docs and a security review.

Nothing has been built or run yet; the only commits are docs.

How do you want slice 1 built?
- **Subagent-driven:** a fresh subagent builds each task and a fresh reviewer checks it before the next one starts. Costs more, but each task gets an independent check.
- **Native:** I build every task in this session, then one reviewer checks the whole branch at the end. Cheaper and faster, but nothing is reviewed independently until the end.

I recommend subagent-driven. The ten tasks pass exact function signatures to each other, and a mistake in access rules or child sign-in would be both a privacy problem and a child-safety problem. A reviewer on each task catches that before the next task builds on it.

Does the plan cover what you want, and which way should we build it?

Task 10 (the end-to-end tests) is running. As you chose, the test setup reads the local keys itself, and nothing is printed. It's built so it can never reach the hosted project:
- The test server is given the local Supabase address and keys directly. These take precedence over `.env.local`.
- The tests abort if they're pointed at anything other than local.
- The test server runs on its own port and never reuses a server you already have running.

**I couldn't push the migrations:** your permission settings blocked `supabase db push`, and I won't work around that. Please run it yourself:

```bash
pnpm db:push
```

It lists the four migrations and asks you to confirm. Once they're applied, the `family_members not found` error goes away.

**The email link problem is confirmed:** the link opened in a different browser than the one you signed up in. Once Task 10 finishes I'll add a **Task 10a**: a `/auth/confirm` page that checks the token in the email link, so the link works from any browser or device. The existing `/auth/callback` stays for OAuth sign-in. Task 10a also updates the local email template. On the hosted project you'll need to update the confirmation email template in the dashboard; I'll give you the exact text when it's done.

**`Date.now()` error:** I'll reproduce it in dev against local Supabase after Task 10. No guesses until then.

Slice 1 (family and onboarding) is built, and every suite passes on the final code:

| Suite | Result |
|---|---|
| `pnpm check` (lint, typecheck, unit tests) | pass |
| Database tests | 150/150 |
| End-to-end tests | 9/9, with no `Date.now()` errors left in the server log |

Each task was reviewed separately, then the whole branch had a general review and a security audit, and every finding from those was fixed.

**Branches:**
- `docs/first-milestone-spec`: 5 commits on `main` (spec, plan, decisions in the docs).
- `feat/family-onboarding`: 43 commits stacked on the docs branch.

## Two problems left that need your decision

These came up in the final re-review. The process allows only one round of fixes after the final review, so they come to you.

1. **Signing in on one device signs the child out on their other devices.** To close the child self-set-password hole, the child's hidden password is replaced at every PIN sign-in. Supabase signs a user out everywhere whenever their password changes; the re-review confirmed this in Supabase's source. The choices:
   - Replace it only on PIN reset, and rely on the "Secure password change" setting on hosted to block self-set passwords. This is my recommendation.
   - Accept that a child can only stay signed in on one device.
2. **One person can block `/start` for everyone for a day.** Requests that the per-IP limit refuses still count toward the daily cap of 200 invitations for the whole app. So one person on one IP can use up the cap. The fix is to check the overall cap only after the per-IP and per-email limits pass. It's small.

## Decisions I made for you during the build

Each with what it costs if I got it wrong:
- **PIN check reports a status instead of raising an error.** Raising would undo the wrong-PIN counter, so the lockout would never trigger. Cost: none.
- **Lockout redesigned twice.** First it got longer with each lock. Then, after the security audit, it applies per child and per device or network, plus a cap of 100 wrong PINs a day across all devices, and every lockout is recorded. Cost: children behind one shared school network count as one source.
- **Child sign-in rate limits:** per IP, per family code and IP combined, and per parent for invitation lookups. Cost: legitimate retries get slowed down.
- **The accepting parent's email doesn't have to match the invitation.** Handing over the link or code in person is the main path, so a different parent accepting is normal. Cost: anyone holding the code can claim the invitation.
- **Added Task 6a, the design foundation from `docs/design.md`,** which had never been applied. Cost: one extra task.
- **Darkened the error red and the input border** to meet the contrast levels `docs/design.md` itself requires, and updated `docs/design.md`. Cost: slightly darker than the designer chose.
- **The parent's actual consent answer is passed to the database,** so its consent check really applies. Cost: none.
- **Sign-up and the confirmation email carry the invitation** through to the end. Cost: edits to the auth code outside Task 8's planned files.
- **Added `/auth/confirm`** so confirmation links work from any browser. Cost: you need to change a hosted email template.
- **Hardened `safeNextPath` against hidden characters** (the open redirect). Cost: none.
- **Signed-out visits to `/child` go to child sign-in, not the parent login.** Cost: none.
- **Client IP for rate limits comes from `x-forwarded-for`.** Vercel sets this header itself. Cost: on any other host, IP limits can be bypassed.
- **The child's hidden password is replaced on sign-in and on PIN reset,** and a PIN reset signs the child out everywhere. Cost: this caused problem 1 above.
- **Minor implementation choices:**
  - invitation tokens in hex
  - all route constants added in Task 6
  - `.env.example` added to without being read
  - tasks run one after another
  - screens checked by build plus the end-to-end tests

## What you need to do on the hosted project

1. Delete the leftover test users. In Authentication → Users, search for `smoke` (the `smoke…@gmail.com` user) and `child.jarso`, and delete any matches.
2. Run `pnpm db:push`. There are now 6 migrations, and pushing resets any current lockouts. Afterwards, check that a PIN reset works; it needs permission to delete Supabase sessions.
3. Paste the Confirm signup email template from `docs/auth.md` → "Hosted setup".
4. Under URL Configuration, add `<app URL>/auth/callback**` to the redirect URLs.
5. Turn on "Secure password change", and keep the password-strength rules at their defaults, or every child sign-in will fail.

The smaller leftover issues are listed in the ledger and the review reports.

Slice 1 is complete. What would you like to do?

1. Merge back to main locally (docs branch, then the feature branch)
2. Push and create pull requests (docs PR into `main`, then the feature PR stacked on it)
3. Keep the branches as they are (I'll handle it later)

Which option?

chars: 940

The PIN is **4 to 6 digits, numbers only**, and you choose it when you add the child. Both the form and the database enforce that.

- **What to pick:** something the child can remember but a sibling or classmate can't guess. A 6-digit PIN is much harder to guess than a 4-digit one. Avoid `1234`, `0000`, repeated digits and birthdays. The app doesn't block weak PINs yet, so that's on you.
- **Why the length matters:** the PIN is the child's only sign-in. The child signs in with the family code, their nickname and the PIN, and a wrong PIN counts toward a lockout. So a guessable PIN is the main risk.
- **Changing it:** the family page has "Reset PIN" for each child. A reset also signs the child out on every device.
- **For a local test:** any 4–6 digits work. The end-to-end tests use `1234` and `2468`.

I can add a short blocklist of common PINs (`1234`, `0000`, `1111` and the like) to the add-child and reset forms if you'd like.

timestamp: 2026-10-28T16:32:59.741Z

I'd need six questions answered before planning this. They're ranked by importance below.

1. **Are savings deposits in this milestone or not?**
   - The spec lists deposits as a non-goal (§2).
   - But §2 also promises a test for every first-milestone acceptance criterion. REQUIREMENTS.md lists one of those criteria as "A savings deposit posts only after the parent confirms receiving the cash. Cancellation and rejection change nothing."
   - **Why it matters:** if deposits are in, slice 4 grows. It would need the deposit type, a cancel transition, a Spend-cap check at both request and confirmation, child and parent screens, and notices. If they're out, slice 0 has to edit that acceptance criterion and the slice 6 checklist changes.

2. **How do judges see an allowance fall due and get a reminder during a live demo?**
   - Generation runs hourly and reminders fire every 3 days (D4), all in family-timezone dates.
   - A live demo needs either a pre-aged seed, a "run now" trigger, or a clock override (functions taking a `now` parameter).
   - The demo runs on the one hosted project, which previews also share. The spec also lists admin tooling as a non-goal.
   - **Why it matters:** it decides whether every time-based function takes an injectable clock. It also decides whether a guarded trigger route ships to production, and what slice 6's seed must look like.

3. **What exists for a child-initiated onboarding before the parent approves?**
   - Architecture §7.1 creates the child's auth user only in `accept_invitation`, so before approval there's no account or session at all. The spec instead implies a persistent "waiting for your parent" screen and a testable "unapproved child cannot use app".
   - Do we need a pre-approval child identity (for example an invited `family_members` row), or is the waiting screen a stateless confirmation?
   - If the invited parent already has a family, does the child join that family or start a new one?
   - **Why it matters:** this shapes the slice 1 schema (nullable `family_id` on invitations, member statuses), the RLS tests, and the email-spam surface. The child form is unauthenticated and sends email to any address, so it needs rate limiting.

4. **Is there a sending domain and Resend account for the hosted demo?**
   - Resend can't send to arbitrary recipients without a verified domain, and `jarso.vercel.app` can't be verified.
   - Supabase's built-in SMTP is heavily rate-limited for parent sign-up confirmation (D7).
   - Child-initiated onboarding depends on email in slice 1, not slice 5, so the "ship without email" fallback in §10 doesn't cover it.
   - **Why it matters:** without email, the slice 1 critical path needs a non-email fallback for the invitation link (a short code, or a copyable link).

5. **Can a parent have more than one child in this milestone?**
   - REQUIREMENTS.md still lists this as an open decision, and the spec doesn't resolve it. The schema supports many children (ADR 0005), but the parent routes in architecture §4.2 have no child selector.
   - **Why it matters:** it decides whether every parent screen, action and badge is scoped by a child id (child switcher, route params) or assumes a single child. Retrofitting that later touches every parent screen.

6. **Which currency, and how many decimal places?**
   - `families.currency` exists, but pilot countries are still open. Hosting in Tokyo hints at JPY, which has no decimal places.
   - **Why it matters:** `formatMinor`, amount input parsing, the Zod schemas, the seed values and the test fixtures all depend on how many minor units make one main unit. The plan also needs to know whether the parent picks the currency at family creation or it's fixed.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every file listed in the brief has its hunk. The three helper signatures match the brief and the global-constraints interface:
  - `tests.create_user(email, role default 'parent')` returns uuid and writes `jarso_role` into `raw_app_meta_data` (`_helpers.psql:104-120`).
  - `tests.authenticate_as(uuid)` is at `_helpers.psql:122-134`.
  - `tests.clear_auth()` is at `_helpers.psql:136-144`.
- `package.json` has `db:test`, and `ci.yml:30-31` runs `supabase test db --local` after the reset. The `docs/testing.md` DB row and section are updated, and the docs note that `\ir` works.
- `harness.test.sql` matches Step 1: it plans 2 assertions, checks `auth.uid()`, then checks `current_user = 'postgres'` after `clear_auth`.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: `pnpm db:test` failing non-zero on a failed assertion. The report says RED exited 1 (the helpers were missing, so no plan ran). That does not show a failed `is()` also exits non-zero. `supabase test db` runs `pg_prove`, which does exit non-zero on a failed assertion, so this is very likely fine. If you want it proven, the one cheap check is to flip an expected value in `harness.test.sql` and run `pnpm db:test`.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: the `trigger created the profile` claim. The harness never asserts it, and the trigger lives in earlier migrations.

### Strengths

- The helpers use `\ir` inside the test transaction, so the schema, the functions, and the pgTAP extension all roll back with the test and nothing persists.
- `authenticate_as` uses `set_config(..., true)` and `set local role`, which are transaction-scoped. That matches how Supabase RLS reads `request.jwt.claims`.
- The role goes only through `raw_app_meta_data`, which matches invariant 15.
- No credentials appear in any committed file.
- The CI step sits between the reset and the lint, and the Supabase version is already pinned.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `_helpers.psql:102`: `create schema if not exists tests` is a no-op if a `tests` schema already exists, and `create or replace function` would then overwrite whatever was there. This is harmless here, so no fix is needed.
- `_helpers.psql:104-120`: `create_user` does not validate `role`. A typo such as `'prent'` would silently create a user with an unknown role. Later tasks that depend on role checks could be misled, so consider `assert role in ('parent','child')` if those are the only valid values.
- `_helpers.psql:142`: `clear_auth` sets the claims to `''`. This works because `auth.uid()` uses `nullif`, so there is no change needed. Use `set_config('request.jwt.claims', null, true)` only if you want to avoid relying on that.
- `harness.test.sql`: it does not assert that the profile trigger fired for a created user. Task 2 will probably cover this, so it is optional here.
- Report note: `pnpm db:start` printed local demo keys into the implementer's session output. `.claude-logs/` is public in this repo, so confirm the log redaction caught them. They are local-demo keys, so the risk is low.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The harness is small, transaction-scoped, and matches the brief and the interface later tasks need. The one thing I could not confirm is a failing assertion producing a non-zero exit, and `pg_prove` very likely does that.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every interface in the brief is in the diff:
  - `profiles.role`/`nickname` columns, with `handle_new_user` reading role from `raw_app_meta_data` (migration L8-32).
  - Column-level update grant on `full_name`, `avatar_url` (L36-37).
  - `families`, `family_members` and `audit_log` with the brief's columns, checks and PK/unique (L43-111).
  - The four predicates as `stable security definer set search_path = ''` (L125-189).
  - `create_family` with JA401/JA403/JA409, a 6-char code from the alphabet, a parent membership and a `family.created` audit row (L195-273).
  - `revoke ... from public, anon` and `grant ... to authenticated` (L277-291).
  - The four select policies, with no write policies (L297-329).
  - All 8 brief test assertions are in `family_core.test.sql`. Lines: code regex L26, JA409 L35, JA403 L50, PB isolation L55-57, PX L83, role/full_name grant L40-42, user_metadata spoof L15-19, direct insert L36-39. The plan count of 35 matches the assertions written.
  - `docs/database.md` Current schema table is updated (diff L28-37).
- Deviation, accepted: there is no separate `family_members(family_id)` index. The PK `(family_id, profile_id)` leads with `family_id` (migration L77, comment L84), so a second index would be redundant.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: L13's comment says nickname is "unique per family", but no constraint enforces it. The controller should confirm a later task (register_child) adds that uniqueness. If none does, the comment needs fixing.

### Strengths

- Invariant 15 holds:
  - Table-level UPDATE on `profiles` is revoked and only `(full_name, avatar_url)` is re-granted (L36-37).
  - Role comes only from `raw_app_meta_data`, and unknown values fall back to `parent` (L28).
  - The test covers a `user_metadata` spoof (test L15-19), a role update (L40) and a nickname update (L41).
- TRUNCATE is revoked from anon/authenticated on `profiles` and the new tables (L36, L115-116). This is a good catch: RLS does not apply to TRUNCATE, and Supabase's default grants would otherwise allow it.
- The security definer predicates cannot be abused:
  - Each one only answers questions about `auth.uid()`'s own membership.
  - `is_parent_of(x)` returns true only when x is an approved child in the caller's own approved-parent family (L148-158).
  - None of them takes a caller-controlled family/profile pair that could reveal another family's data.
- Grants to anon on the new tables and functions are revoked, and there is a test for it (test L91-93).
- `create_family`:
  - Validates the timezone against `pg_timezone_names`.
  - Generates the code uniformly (256 % 32 = 0) with a bounded retry on collision.
  - Maps the concurrent same-parent race on `profile_id unique` to JA409 (L254-260). The whole transaction rolls back, so no orphan family row is left.
- RLS and helper style is good: the policies use `(select auth.uid())` and `(select public.current_family_id())` (L310). Functions use schema-qualified names, including `pg_catalog.*`. RLS is enabled in the same migration.
- The tests go past the brief: cross-family `is_parent_of` and child-profile visibility (L74-81), a child cannot read the audit log (L77), and the consent check constraint (L64-66).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **Parent account deletion will fail.** `families.created_by` (L48), `family_members.consent_by` and `approved_by` (L74-76) are FKs to `profiles` with no `on delete` action. `profiles.id` cascades from `auth.users` (create_profiles.sql L4), so deleting a parent who created a family or consented for a child will fail on these FKs. That is fine while no account-deletion flow exists. When one is added, it needs either `on delete set null` (making the columns nullable) or a function that cleans these rows up first.

2. **A removed parent can never create a new family.** `create_family`'s JA409 check counts any membership row, including `status = 'removed'` (L223). The brief says "no membership", so this is compliant, and `profile_id unique` would block a re-insert anyway. It is a product decision worth recording if removal is ever expected to free a parent to start over.

3. **Test L36-39 no longer tests RLS.** The test is labelled "blocked by RLS", but 42501 now comes from the revoked INSERT grant (L116), so RLS is never reached. If a future migration re-grants INSERT, nothing would show whether RLS still blocks the write. Fix by relabelling the test, or by adding an assertion that `family_members` has no insert/update/delete policies, e.g. `policies_are` or a query on `pg_policies`.

4. **Brief-mandated redundancy in the profiles policy.** The `is_parent_of(id)` clause (L307) is a subset of the `current_family_id()` clause (L308-313). The brief mandates it and the performance cost is negligible at pilot scale, but it adds a per-row security-definer call.

5. **`current_family_id()` should be nullable in the generated types.** It returns null for users without a family, but `database.types.ts` most likely types it as a non-null `string` (CLI limitation). Callers should treat it as `string | null`.

6. **Nickname comment claims an unenforced constraint.** See the ⚠️ item above (migration L13).

Checks run, each against a named risk:

- **`tests.create_user(email, 'child')` signature.** Checked `supabase/tests/database/_helpers.psql`: it accepts `role` and writes it to `raw_app_meta_data`. OK.
- **`tests.clear_auth()` actually returns to postgres for the as-owner child insert (test L62-64).** Checked the same file: it runs `reset role`. OK.
- **The old `profiles` policies conflict with the new ones.** Checked `supabase/migrations/20261028000000_create_profiles.sql` L15-24:
  - The only policies were owner select and owner update with check.
  - The owner select policy is dropped and replaced (migration L297).
  - The owner update policy is kept and works together with the column grant.
  - There is no insert/delete policy, so the remaining INSERT/DELETE grants on `profiles` stay blocked by RLS.
- **Regenerated types are wrong.** Grepped `src/types/database.types.ts`: `audit_log`, `families`, `family_members`, `profiles.role`/`nickname`, `create_family`, `current_family_id` and `is_parent_of` are present, with the correct FK relationships. Formatting not judged.
- **Account deletion is in scope.** Grepped `REQUIREMENTS.md` and `docs/domain.md` for deletion requirements. No account-deletion requirement, so Minor 1 stays Minor.

I did not re-run the test suite. The implementer reports 37/37 (35 here plus 2 from Task 1), db lint clean and `pnpm check` passing. The plan count is consistent with the assertions in the file.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The schema, grants and RLS match the brief and the migration rules. Family isolation and server-only role assignment are enforced at both the grant and the policy level, and the tests prove it. The remaining items are about future features (account deletion, re-joining) and test clarity, not current correctness or security.

timestamp: 2026-10-28T18:28:02.545Z

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant, including both controller rulings.
  - Ruling (a): `verify_child_pin` returns `table(child_id uuid, status text)` (migration.sql:404-467). Unknown family/nickname, no credentials, a removed child or a wrong PIN all return `(null,'invalid')`. A locked account returns `(null,'locked')`. A correct PIN returns `(id,'ok')`. The function never raises, so the failure counter persists. Test lines 661-670 assert `4/none` after four failures, which proves no rollback.
  - Ruling (b): `register_child` has the exact signature (p_child_id, p_family_id, p_nickname, p_pin, p_consent, p_invitation_id default null) and ignores `p_invitation_id` (migration.sql:271-348, comment at :321). The types file matches (`database.types.ts:178-187`).
  - Brief requirements, all present:
    - `child_credentials` with RLS on and no policies (:225-245).
    - `nickname_key` plus the partial unique index `(family_id, nickname_key) where role='child'` (:212-219).
    - `usage_counters` (:251-261).
    - `set_child_pin` uses `is_parent_of`, resets the counters and audits `child.pin_reset` (:356-394).
    - `hit_rate_limit` uses `date_bin(p_window, now(), 'epoch')` with an atomic upsert-returning (:475-497).
    - Grants: verify and rate-limit are service_role only; register and set are authenticated only; anon is revoked (:505-525).
    - bcrypt goes through `extensions.crypt(..., gen_salt('bf',10))`, as the global constraint requires.
    - Every brief test bullet is present in `child_accounts.test.sql` (44 assertions).
  - Deviation, acceptable: an invalid nickname length raises `22023` rather than `JA409` (:312-314). The brief is ambiguous here, `22023` is the semantically correct code, and docs/database.md:41 records it.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - That the service layer (later task) maps `invalid`→UNAUTHORIZED and `locked`→RATE_LIMITED, and applies IP or per-family `hit_rate_limit` before calling `verify_child_pin`.
  - That Task 4 binds `p_invitation_id` so a parent can only claim the child profile tied to their invitation (see Minor 4).

### Strengths

- Lockout under concurrency is correct. `select ... for update` on the credentials row (:427-430) serialises concurrent attempts. Under READ COMMITTED, a waiting transaction re-reads the latest `failed_attempts` and `locked_until` after acquiring the lock, so parallel guesses cannot slip past the counter.
- The definer functions use `search_path = ''` with qualified names.
- `register_child` requires the caller to be an approved parent of `p_family_id` specifically, not just any parent (:293-302). The test shows PB→family A is refused (test:600-603), as are child callers (test:605-608) and a parent-role profile registered as a child (test:596-598).
- `set_child_pin` uses `is_parent_of`. I checked family_core.sql:143-161: it requires the caller to be an approved parent and the child to be approved in the same family, so a cross-family reset is impossible (test:712-713).
- Unique-violation races (same child registered twice, or a nickname clash) map to `JA409` (:323-335).
- On an unknown nickname, `verify_child_pin` runs a dummy bcrypt at the same cost (:433-438), so response time does not reveal whether a nickname exists.
- The PIN never reaches `audit_log` (asserted at test:567-569).
- Anon table grants are revoked, and the brief's 0-rows/42501 expectations are tested (test:617-624).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **Lockout resets the counter, so brute force runs indefinitely at 5 guesses per 15 minutes** (migration.sql:454-458). On the 5th failure the code sets `failed_attempts = 0`. That sustains 480 guesses per day per child: about 10 days on average to crack a 4-digit PIN, or about 2.6 years for a 6-digit one, while the real child stays locked out the whole time.
   - This matches the global constraint ("5 failures, 15 minutes"), so it is not a spec violation.
   - Recommendation for the controller, spec level: add escalating lockout (for example, double `locked_until` on each consecutive lock and reset only on success or `set_child_pin`), and/or notify the parent on lock. At minimum, make sure the service layer rate-limits per IP and per family code with `hit_rate_limit` before calling `verify_child_pin`.

2. **Lockout works as a nickname-existence oracle and a targeted lockout tool** (:440-443, report concern 5). Someone holding a family code can send 5 wrong PINs for a guessed nickname. If the next response is `locked`, the nickname exists. The same move locks out a real child.
   - This is inherent to per-account lockout. It is listed so the controller consciously accepts it and relies on the per-IP rate limit.

3. **Child rows can have a NULL `nickname_key`, which would bypass the uniqueness index** (:212-219). The check only forces `nickname_key` to be null for non-child rows. NULLs are distinct in a unique index, so any future child insert path (for example Task 4) that forgets `nickname_key` produces rows that dodge uniqueness and can never sign in.
   - Fix: `check ((role = 'child') = (nickname_key is not null))`.

4. **`register_child` will claim any orphan child-role profile whose UUID the caller supplies** (:316-319). Today the only child-role profiles without a membership are the transient ones the server creates just before calling this function, and UUIDs are unguessable, so the risk is low.
   - Task 4 should bind the child id to the invitation rather than trusting a caller-supplied `p_child_id`.

5. **A stale credentials row surfaces as a raw `23505`** (:339-340). The credentials insert sits outside the `unique_violation` handler. If a `child_credentials` row already exists for a child with no membership (only possible after manual deletion of the membership), the raw unique violation is returned instead of `JA409`.
   - Fix: use `on conflict (profile_id) do update`, as `set_child_pin` does, or move the insert inside the handler block.

6. **One error message covers two causes** (:334). "Nickname already used in this family" is also raised for a concurrent duplicate registration of the same child. The code (`JA409`) is right; only the message misleads.

7. **`hit_rate_limit` lets a raw error through for month or year windows** (:485-490). It validates `p_window > 0` but not months or years, so `date_bin` raises its own error ("timestamps cannot be binned into intervals containing months or years"). It is service-only, so low impact.

8. **Two tables deviate from the docs/database.md migration rules.** Both deviations are plan-mandated by the brief's table shapes and single-file layout, so they are not defects of the implementer.
   - `usage_counters` lacks `created_at` and `updated_at` (rule 6).
   - The migration bundles the rate limiter with child accounts (rule 1, "one logical change").

9. **Test gaps.**
   - No assertion that `anon` cannot execute `register_child`, `set_child_pin` or `verify_child_pin`. The grants at :505-510 look correct, but nothing tests them.
   - No window-rollover case for `hit_rate_limit`.
   - The bare `select public.verify_child_pin(...)` calls at test:665-667 and :700 print non-TAP rows into the pg_prove output. The run passes per the report, but `perform`-style wrapping (for example `select count(*) from (...)`) keeps the output clean.

10. **Authenticated keeps SELECT on both no-policy tables** (:264-265), along with REFERENCES and TRIGGER. RLS returns 0 rows, so nothing is exposed. `revoke all ... from authenticated` would be stricter defence in depth and would not change any brief test that expects 0 rows. Optional.

Checks I ran outside the diff:
- **Risk:** `set_child_pin` letting a parent reset a child outside their family. **Checked:** `is_parent_of` in `supabase/migrations/20261028181734_family_core.sql:143-161`. It requires an approved parent and an approved child in the same family, so this is safe.
- **Checked:** the docs/database.md migration rules (lines 28-39) against the migration. Result: findings 8 and 10.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The migration matches the brief and both controller rulings. The lockout is concurrency-safe through a row lock and never-raise semantics, and the definer functions authorise against the specific family, with grants that keep the credentials and verifier away from clients. What remains is hardening: the brute-force ceiling and nickname oracle, the NULL `nickname_key` invariant, and the raw `23505` on a stale credentials row.

Additional scope (controller ruling — do it in the same new migration, since migrations are forward-only; add tests for each in child_accounts.test.sql or a new hardening section):
A. Escalating PIN lockout. Add `child_credentials.lock_count int not null default 0`. On the 5th consecutive failure: lock_count += 1, locked_until = now() + least(interval '15 minutes' * 2^(lock_count-1), interval '24 hours'), and failed_attempts resets to 0 (so the next lock needs 5 more failures). lock_count resets to 0 ONLY on a successful verify or set_child_pin. B. `family_members` check constraint: `(role = 'child') = (nickname_key is not null)` (replace the existing weaker check if there is one). Test a child row with null nickname_key is rejected.
C. register_child's child_credentials insert becomes `on conflict (profile_id) do update` (fresh hash, counters/lock reset, set_by) so a stale row can't surface a raw 23505. Test it.
D. Remove the "unused p_invitation_id" lint warning by using it (your main task does this).

Notes for the main task:
- Invitations: register_child(p_child_id, p_family_id, p_nickname, p_pin, p_consent, p_invitation_id) is `create or replace` with the SAME signature; invitation branch locks the row for update, requires pending + unexpired (else JA404), marks accepted with accepted_by=auth.uid(), child_id, family_id, all in the same transaction as the membership insert.

- create_invitation is service_role only. invitations: RLS on, no policies, anon/authenticated revoked.

Tests: Before the migration, the new tests failed: `lock_count` did not exist and `create_invitation` did not exist. After `pnpm db:reset && pnpm db:test`, all 121 tests in 4 files pass. That covers invitations (24, new) and child_accounts (60, up from 44). `supabase db lint --local --fail-on error` reports no errors and no warnings. `pnpm db:types` was regenerated, `docs/database.md` updated, and `pnpm check` passes.

Change you didn't list: `family_core.test.sql` inserted child rows without a `nickname_key`, which constraint B now rejects. I updated those fixtures, so the "requires consent" test now fails only on missing consent.

Concerns:
1. Constraint B drops the old check by its auto-generated name, `family_members_check1`. This is reliable because migrations always replay in order, but it breaks if that earlier migration is ever edited.
2. Accepting an invitation does not check that the accepting parent's email matches `parent_email`, or that the nickname matches the invitation's. Holding the link or code is enough. The brief doesn't ask for either check.
3. `find_invitation` has no rate limit in the database. The service layer must rate-limit code lookups, because short codes can be guessed at scale.
4. Nothing ever sets `status` to `'expired'`; expiry is decided by `expires_at` alone.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. All brief items and controller scope A–D are implemented and tested.
  - **Table:** the `invitations` table matches the brief (migration lines 27–40). RLS is on with no policies. All privileges are revoked from anon; insert/update/delete/truncate are revoked from authenticated (lines 53–57).
  - **create_invitation:** service_role only (lines 355–363). Expiry is 7 days (line 84).
  - **find_invitation:** matches token_hash or code_hash, pending and unexpired only, returns id+nickname only (lines 97–109). Granted to authenticated, revoked from anon.
  - **register_child:** the signature is unchanged. It takes a `for update` lock on the pending, unexpired invitation and raises JA404 otherwise (lines 167–179). It marks the invitation accepted with accepted_by, child_id and family_id in the same transaction (lines 213–220).
  - **A:** `lock_count int not null default 0` is added. The lock duration is `15min * 2^least(old,7)` capped at 24h (line 334). Since old = new−1, this equals 15 min × 2^(n−1). On lock, failed_attempts resets to 0. lock_count resets only on success (line 320) and in set_child_pin (line 264). Tests cover 15/30/60 min, the 24h cap, and both resets.
  - **B:** the named check `(role='child') = (nickname_key is not null)` is in place. Both directions are tested (23514).
  - **C:** `on conflict (profile_id) do update` resets the hash, set_by and all counters. Tested with a stale row, and the new PIN verifies.
  - **D:** p_invitation_id is used.
  - **verify_child_pin:** still returns `table(child_id, status)` and has no new raise paths.
  - **Tests the brief asked for:** all present in invitations.test.sql: find by token and by code, unknown hash, accept, reuse → JA404 plus find empty, expired (both find and register), and authenticated getting 42501 on create plus 0 rows on select.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff:
  - **Invitation binding:** acceptance is not bound to `parent_email` or to the invitation nickname. Any approved parent holding the id can accept it. The brief doesn't require binding; the controller should confirm the product intent (implementer flagged it too).
  - **Rate limiting:** has to happen in the service layer (later task).

### Checks run

- **Risk: dropping `family_members_check1` might drop the wrong constraint (the consent check).**
  - Checked: family_core.sql:80 defines the unnamed table-level consent check, which gets the auto-name `family_members_check`.
  - Checked: child_accounts.sql:10 defines the nickname_key column check. It references two columns, so it is auto-named `family_members_check1`.
  - Result: the drop targets the right constraint. The family_core "requires consent" test still exercises the consent check.
- **Risk: replaced function bodies silently changing Task 3 behaviour.**
  - Checked: diffed the bodies of set_child_pin, verify_child_pin and register_child against 20261028182342_child_accounts.sql.
  - Result: the only differences are the lock_count additions, the invitation branch and the upsert.
  - `create or replace` keeps the existing grants and comments.
- **Race review: double accept.** Under READ COMMITTED, a second accept blocks on `FOR UPDATE` at line 174. After the first commits, Postgres re-checks the waiting row against the WHERE clause (EvalPlanQual), sees `status='accepted'`, `found` is false, and the second call gets JA404. That is correct.
  - Failure path: a JA409 nickname clash rolls back the whole function, so the invitation stays pending (tested).

### Strengths

- The invitation lock is taken before the membership insert, and the status/expiry predicates live in the locked query. Replay and concurrent accepts are both closed.
- The exponent cap (`least(lock_count, 7)`) prevents interval overflow, and the lock_count=1000 test proves it.
- The tests use exact `now()` equality inside the transaction for deterministic duration checks.
- Grants are explicit. Their comment explains Supabase's default function grants.
- FK indexes are present.
- Docs are updated precisely.
- Generated types are correct: new `lock_count`; `invitations` Row/Insert/Update, with `expires_at` required on insert and kind/status optional; `create_invitation` / `find_invitation` signatures.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **find_invitation can be called directly over PostgREST** (`supabase/migrations/20261028182920_invitations.sql:362`).
   - The grant is to `authenticated`, which includes child-role users. Any signed-in user can call `rpc('find_invitation')` directly, so a service-layer rate limit can be bypassed.
   - Risk is low: the 8-char code gives 32^8 ≈ 1.1e12 possibilities, and each call checks one hash.
   - The brief mandates `authenticated`, so this is advisory. If the service layer is meant to be the only lookup path, grant it to service_role only, or call `hit_rate_limit` inside the function.
2. **create_invitation does not validate `parent_email`** (line 84).
   - An empty string after btrim is accepted, with no format or length check. The same goes for the hash arguments (no shape or length check).
   - The caller is service_role only, so the Zod layer will cover it. A cheap `check (parent_email <> '')` or `char_length` bound would add depth.
3. **One lockout test asserts too little** (`supabase/tests/database/child_accounts.test.sql:219`).
   - "attempts while locked do not count" asserts only the `locked` status. It does not check that failed_attempts and lock_count are unchanged.
   - Later assertions cover this indirectly (`1/4/true` after expiry).
4. **`status = 'expired'` is never written.** Expiry is purely time-based. This is consistent across find and register, but `status` can show `pending` for dead rows. Document it, or add a sweeper later.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** Every brief requirement and controller item A–D is implemented as specified and covered by pgTAP tests. Invitation single-use, the expiry check and grants are correct. The remaining points are defence-in-depth and test-tightening polish.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every listed file has a hunk. All brief test cases are present: normalise, generateCode(6) over 100 runs, hash length and stability, childEmail, JA423, unknown code to INTERNAL with message "f", PIN rejections, consent missing, bad email, "Mars/Base".
- Schemas match the DB rules: nickname trimmed 1–20 (schemas.ts:196-200), PIN `^\d{4,6}$` (:202), currency enum (:218).
- JA401/403/404/409/423/429 and 22023 are all mapped (db-error.ts:305-313).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff: `generateToken` returns 64-char hex (codes.ts:95-98). The global constraints say "32 random bytes base64url". The brief only says `generateToken(): string`, and the entropy is the same (256 bits). Only the encoding differs, and the hash is computed in TS, so this is safe as long as no DB code assumes base64url. The controller should check that Task 6+ code treats the token as opaque.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff: the brief says "Always sets `cause`". The `cause` is set to `error`, which is null when `error` is null. This is fine.

### Strengths
- `codes.ts` is isomorphic, with no `import` of crypto. The comment correctly notes that the 32-char alphabet divides 256, so `b % 32` has no modulo bias.
- The family barrel exports only client-safe modules, with no server-only imports. That follows the CLAUDE.md barrel rule.
- Error messages stay generic and do not leak raw DB text. The raw error is kept only in `cause`.
- Checked `errors.ts` for a named risk: `AppError` accepts `{cause}`, and the `ErrorCode` set contains every mapped code, so the types are sound.
- The SHA-256 test asserts a known vector, so it checks real behavior rather than self-consistency.
- `childSignInSchema.pin` is only non-empty. This avoids leaking the PIN format, which is a reasonable choice.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `codes.ts:94` — the doc comment says "URL-safe random token (hex)" while the global constraint says base64url. Either switch to base64url or note the deviation in the plan.
- `schemas.ts:204-206` — consent as `z.union([literal("on"), literal(true)])` gives a union-style issue when the field is missing. The custom `error:` option should cover that. Not verified at runtime, but the test at schemas.test.ts:148-151 only asserts `success: false`, not the message. Low risk.
- `schemas.ts:219` — `isValidTimezone` accepts anything `Intl` accepts. Some engines accept aliases such as "utc", or lowercase variants. The DB may be stricter. Low risk.
- `db-error.ts:305` — `Record<string, …>` indexed by an arbitrary `error.code` string like "constructor" or "toString" would hit `Object.prototype` and return a truthy non-entry. A PostgREST code would never be that, but `Object.hasOwn` or a `Map` is safer. Very low risk.
- The report says "no separate RED run". The brief called for a RED step, so TDD evidence is absent. This is a process note, not a code defect.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The implementation matches the brief and the DB rules, keeps the barrel client-safe, and tests real behavior. Only minor polish items remain, plus the hex vs base64url token encoding deviation to confirm downstream.

timestamp: 2026-10-28T18:44:38.968Z
time: 2026-10-28 18:44
chars: 7061

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant, with the controller rulings applied. All files in the brief are present. `session.ts` is unchanged, which matches the brief's "no change". The route constants are in `src/config/site.ts:12-18` and `protectedRoutePrefixes` is untouched (`site.ts:21`). The rate limits match the rulings: `pin:ip` 20/15 min plus `pin:family:<normalised>` 30/hour, both checked before verify (`service.ts:168-176`); `invite-lookup:user` 10/hour (`service.ts:135-138`); invite limits 5/hour and 3/day (`service.ts:94-100`). The verify statuses map to the exact ruled copy (`service.ts:177-182`). The token is 64-char hex and only its hashes are stored (`service.ts:102-109`; the test asserts `[0-9a-f]{64}`). Email is best effort and does not log PII (`email.ts:14-17`, `service.ts:123-125`).
- ❌ Small deviation from Step 4: `service.ts:5` imports `createAdminClient` and builds the admin client itself (`service.ts:38`, `:104`, `:176`), then passes it into the repository. The brief limits the admin client to `child-auth.ts` plus the three repository functions. Table and RPC names still stay in the repository, so this is layering drift, not a security hole. See Minor 1.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from this diff:
  - How `ip` is derived (header trust, fallback when it is missing). The caller is in Task 7+. If an unknown IP falls back to a shared constant such as `"unknown"`, every such request shares one 20/15 min bucket. If the code trusts a client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For` value, the per-IP limit can be bypassed. The controller should check this in the action layer.
  - `find_invitation` is granted to `authenticated` (`invitations.sql:362`), so any signed-in user, child included, can call it directly and skip the service's 10/hour lookup limit. The 8-char code space (32^8, about 1e12) makes brute force impractical. Whether `find_invitation` itself requires the caller to be a parent is Task 5 territory.
  - Whether two parents racing to accept the same invitation is atomic. That lives inside `register_child`, not in this diff.

### Strengths

- Checks I ran for named risks:
  - Role spoofing: the `profiles.role` fallback comes from `raw_app_meta_data`, never `raw_user_meta_data` (`family_core.sql:15,28`). `createChildAuthUser` sets `app_metadata` (`child-auth.ts:24`).
  - RPC grants: `verify_child_pin`, `hit_rate_limit` and `create_invitation` are service_role only (`child_accounts.sql:309-322`, `invitations.sql:360-363`). `register_child` and `set_child_pin` run as the user (`child_accounts.sql:314`).
- Admin authz before use:
  - `createChildAuthUser` is reached only after `assertRole("parent")` and a fail-fast family-match check (`service.ts:74-79`). `register_child` then re-checks in the database with the parent's own client (`service.ts:78-81`). `register_child` also refuses non-child profiles and child profiles that are already used (`invitations.sql:162-164`).
  - The unauthenticated admin paths (`startChildOnboarding`, `childSignIn`) are gated by rate limits and the PIN check, which is right for those flows.
- Session minting is bound to the verified child. `mintChildSession` only receives `result.childId` from `verify_child_pin` (`service.ts:184`), and the input never carries a child id. `generateLink` does not send email.
- Rate limiting:
  - It fails closed: a `hitRateLimit` error throws (`repository.ts:128`).
  - Every limit is recorded on each attempt via `Promise.all` (`service.ts:37-41`).
  - The family key is normalised, so case and hyphen variants cannot split the bucket.
- `verifyChildPin` throws INTERNAL when the status is unknown or the row is missing (`repository.ts:159-162`), so nothing defaults to "ok".
- `sendEmail` keeps only the HTTP status in the error cause (`email.ts:27`), has a 10 s timeout, and logs neither recipient nor body.
- Tests cover every case the brief asks for, using real hashing and exact key assertions (`service.test.ts:45-193`).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **Admin client built in the service** (`service.ts:5,38,104,176`)
   - This is the Step 4 deviation noted above.
   - Fix: either let the three admin repository functions create the client internally, or record the pattern as accepted.
2. **Orphan auth user on an ambiguous create failure** (`child-auth.ts:17-27`)
   - The uuid is generated before the create call. If `createUser` returns an error after GoTrue has already saved the user (timeout or network drop), no cleanup runs and the auth user is orphaned.
   - Fix: on error, best-effort `deleteUser(id)` before throwing. The id is already known, so this is cheap.
3. **Role defaults to the privileged value** (`repository.ts:33-37`, `dal.ts:24-27`)
   - `findProfileRole` returns `"parent"` when no profile row exists.
   - The trigger makes this unlikely, but missing data should not grant the more privileged role.
   - Fix: throw INTERNAL or FORBIDDEN when `data` is null.
4. **DAL redirect and authz logic has no tests** (`dal.ts:36-57`)
   - `requireRole` wrong-role redirects (Review Focus 5) and `assertParentOf` FORBIDDEN have no unit tests; `dal` is fully mocked in `service.test.ts:33-37`.
   - Also untested: the `addChild` family-mismatch FORBIDDEN path, where no auth user should be created (`service.ts:76-77`), plus `acceptInvitation`, `setChildPin` and `createFamily`. The brief did not require these.
5. **Family lockout by anyone who knows the code** (`service.ts:171`)
   - Anyone who knows a family's 6-char code can exhaust the 30/hour `pin:family` bucket and lock every child in that family out for an hour. A single IP can do this in about 16 minutes, given the per-IP limit of 20 per 15 minutes.
   - The controller ruled this limit, so this is noted as an accepted trade-off only.
6. **Double lookup on accept** (`service.ts:152`)
   - `acceptInvitation` calls `findInvitation` again, which spends a second unit of the 10/hour lookup budget. The implementer flagged this too; Task 8 should avoid repeated lookups.
7. **Unauthenticated email with attacker-chosen text** (`service.ts:112-120`)
   - The unauthenticated endpoint sends email to any address, with an attacker-chosen nickname in the subject. The limits (5/hour per IP, 3/day per address) contain the abuse.
   - Fix: keep the nickname length cap strict in the schema.
8. **Signed-out child pages go to the parent login** (`dal.ts:36-37`)
   - Signed-out users hitting child pages are sent to `/login`, not `routes.childSignIn`. This matches the brief, but the child UX is poor.
   - Fix: consider it in Task 9.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The server layer meets the brief and every controller ruling. Admin-client use is gated correctly, session minting is bound to the PIN-verified child, the role comes only from server-set metadata, and orphan cleanup covers the `register_child` failure path. The remaining items are hardening and test-coverage polish, and the IP derivation in the caller layer needs checking in a later task.

1. **Button loading:** new `loading` prop disables the button, sets `aria-busy` and overlays a spinning `Loader2Icon`. The label stays in place with `opacity-0`, so the width holds. 2. **Toasts:**
   - Toasts are bottom-centre, last 4 s, and sit `calc(4rem + env(safe-area-inset-bottom) + 8px)` from the bottom. That is set on both `offset` and `mobileOffset`, because the tab bar stays up to 1024 px while Sonner's mobile breakpoint is 600 px.
   - The border uses `--hairline`.
   - `richColors` is gone; each toast type now shows its icon in a brand colour (primary, accent-text, gold-text, destructive).
3. **Destructive:** light `--destructive` goes from `oklch(0.577 0.245 27.325)` to `oklch(0.56 0.245 27.325)`, same hue. Text on the paper background goes from 4.37:1 to 4.59:1, and white text on a destructive button from 4.76:1 to 5.01:1. docs/design.md §2.4 and §13 are updated with the "darkened to meet §11 AA on paper" note.

Extra fix (not in your list): `--keycap-ledge-gold` was `var(--color-gold-700)`, but Tailwind v4 only outputs theme variables that some class uses. The gold keycap's ledge would have vanished on pages that use no gold-700 class. It is now the literal gold-700 value `#705300`.

- **§13 tokens:** compared line by line against `globals.css:307-447`. Every `:root` and `.dark` value matches; the only differences are Biome's trailing-zero trims (0.970→0.97 and so on). The `@theme inline` additions in §13 are all present (`globals.css:276-297`).
- **Icons** (`file`): `src/app/icon.png` 192×192, `apple-icon.png` 180×180, `favicon.ico` 32×32, `public/brand/icons/jarso-mark-512.png` 512×512 and `-192.png` 192×192. All correct.
- **"SVG mark" in the brief:** `ls public/brand` shows only PNGs (`jarso-mark.png`, `jarso-wordmark.png`). Using a PNG through `next/image` is justified, and §1 forbids redrawing the mark.
- **Removed button sizes (`xs`, `icon-xs`):** grep found no remaining callers.
- **Fonts (§3):** `layout.tsx:547-551` loads Nunito 400/600/700/800 as `--font-sans`, keeps Geist Mono and drops Geist Sans.
- **Type scale (§3):** `globals.css:224-247` matches the table (Display 40/44 800 −0.02em, H1 32/38, H2 24/30, H3 20/26, Money XL/L/Money).
- **Shape (§4):** radius 0.875rem. Card is `rounded-xl` (14 × 1.4 ≈ 20 px). The bottom sheet is `rounded-t-2xl` (≈25 px). Buttons and chips are pills. Input and Select are `rounded-lg` (14 px).
- **Status chips (§2.5):** all six states map exactly to `status-chip.tsx:700-737` (due, pending, approved, handedOver, reserved with dashed border, closed with no red), plus practice in cyan-100.
- **§5 / §11 sizes:**
  - Tab bar `h-16` plus safe-area inset, 24 px icons, `text-xs font-semibold`, gold count badge (`bottom-nav.tsx:883-912`).
  - Header `h-14`, sticky, `bg-background/85`, `backdrop-blur-[16px]`, hairline (`app-header.tsx:787`).
  - 44 px targets: button default/lg/icon `h-11`/`size-11`, Input and Select trigger `h-11`, SelectItem `min-h-11`, checkbox hit area 24 + 2×10 = 44 px, sheet close `icon-lg`, header link `min-h-11`.
- **Cache Components:** `src/app/(app)/layout.tsx:73-90` reads no session.
- **Barrels:** `src/components/ui/index.ts` exports all five new primitives. `@/components/brand` and `@/components/shell` have barrels. `sheet.tsx:1460` imports `./button` relatively.
- **Landing (§14.7):**
  - "I'm a kid" → `routes.childSignIn`, "I'm a parent" → `routes.login` (`page.tsx:137-145`).
  - "New here?" links → `routes.childStart` and `routes.signup` (`page.tsx:153-158`).
  - Two-beat headline; wordmark only on white, hidden in dark per §1 (`page.tsx:122-126`).
- **Contrast:** I spot-checked white on light destructive, which the report doesn't list (`button.tsx:1026`): about 4.77:1, passes.

### Strengths

- The tokens are an exact transcription of §13. The derived extras are clearly marked and grounded in §2.3: `--ink-2`, `--hairline` as a colour, and `--keycap-ledge-gold` defined in both themes.
- The global `:focus-visible` rule (`globals.css:511-514`) replaces the per-primitive `ring-ring/50` and `outline-none`, so §11 "never remove outlines" is handled in one place.
- The reduced-motion block is deliberately left out of any CSS layer (`globals.css:519-528`), so it overrides every Tailwind utility without needing `!important`.
- `has-data-[slot=bottom-nav]` padding keeps the `(app)` layout static and role-agnostic, which fits the Cache Components rule.
- `BottomNav` lights only the deepest matching tab (`bottom-nav.tsx:867-871`), backed by a test that failed before the fix and passes after. Counts use `toLocaleString`, satisfying §11 "never abbreviated", and that is tested too.
- `StatusChip` always pairs an icon with a word, never uses red, and its words follow §9 voice ("Said yes", "Handed over").
- Making 44 px the default button height is a sound, safer reading of §6 and §11.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)

1. **`src/components/ui/button.tsx:1001-1053` has no loading state (§6).** The slice-1 forms (signup, child sign-in, child start) will all submit with a pending state. Without a width-preserving spinner, each screen will invent its own, or the button will just grey out.
   - Fix: add a `loading?: boolean` prop to `Button`. It should render a Lucide `Loader2Icon` with `animate-spin` (reduced motion is already handled globally), set `aria-busy` and `disabled`, and keep the label's width. One way is to keep the label but make it invisible and overlay the spinner. Then use it in `SignOutButton`.
2. **Sonner placement and colours are not adapted (§6 Toast).** See `src/components/providers/index.tsx:11` and `src/components/ui/sonner.tsx:28-33`.
   - On phones, toasts cover the fixed tab bar instead of sitting above it. Sonner's z-index is far above the nav's `z-40`.
   - `richColors` paints the success and info toasts in Sonner's own palette, not the brand tokens.
   - `--normal-border: var(--border)` uses the input border instead of the hairline.
   - Fix: set `position="bottom-center"` and a mobile offset of `calc(4rem + env(safe-area-inset-bottom) + 8px)` when the nav is present (or a constant, since the app shell always has it). Drop `richColors`, or map it to the tokens, and use `--hairline` for the border.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **The keycap keeps its long shadow in dark mode (`src/app/globals.css:464-473`).** §4 and §12 say dark mode "drops the long shadows". The `.keycap` utility always applies `0 8px 16px -10px rgb(20 28 31 / 0.25)` and the white inset highlight. Fix: move the shadow into a `--keycap-shadow` variable that is redefined in `.dark` without the long term.
2. **Awkward accessible name for count tabs (`src/components/shell/bottom-nav.tsx:898-912`).** The badge, with its sr-only " new", renders before the label, so screen readers announce "1,250 new Inbox". Put the label first, or give the link an explicit `aria-label`, e.g. `Inbox, 1,250 new`.
3. **Inconsistent overlap of the `lg` rail (`bottom-nav.tsx:883`).** The rail uses `lg:top-14`, but the header is `h-14` plus `pt-[env(safe-area-inset-top)]`. On an iPad-width installed PWA with a top inset, the rail will start under the header. Use `lg:top-[calc(3.5rem+env(safe-area-inset-top))]`.
4. **Weak test (`src/components/brand/status-chip.test.tsx:676-679`).** "Never uses destructive" only checks that the class string has no `destructive`. That is acceptable as a guard but does not prove the §2.5 tone. Optionally, assert the `data-status`, and that the tone classes are `bg-muted text-muted-foreground`.
5. **Input border at rest is low contrast** (`#E6E2DA` on `#EFECE5`, `input.tsx:1193`). The implementer already flagged this as a design.md question, and I agree it belongs to the designer rather than this task. Noted here so it isn't lost.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The design foundation closely follows docs/design.md, with exact tokens and the type, shape, layout and accessibility rules applied, and the Cache Components and barrel rules are respected. Two explicit §6 component requirements are still missing, though, and the slice-1 screens will depend on both: the Button loading state, and toasts placed above the tab bar.

   - `src/features/auth/components/sign-out-button.tsx:11` passes `loading={pending}`.
   - `src/components/ui/button.test.tsx` has 3 tests: busy and disabled, label retained with spinner present, and the plain state. The report shows `pnpm check` with 58/58 passing. I did not re-run the suite.
2. **Sonner placement and brand styling (§6 Toast)** — ADDRESSED. All in `src/components/ui/sonner.tsx`:
   - `position="bottom-center"` and `duration={4000}`.
   - `offset` and `mobileOffset` are both `calc(4rem + env(safe-area-inset-bottom) + 8px)`. `node_modules/sonner/dist/index.d.ts:112-113` confirms both props exist.
   - `--normal-border` is `var(--hairline)`.
   - `richColors` is removed in `src/components/providers/index.tsx`. Type icons use brand tokens.
   - The report says no live toast was triggered, which is acceptable since no slice-1 page fires one yet.
3. **Light `--destructive` at least 4.5:1 on paper and white-on-destructive at least 4.5:1** — ADDRESSED.
   - `src/app/globals.css` changes light `--destructive` to `oklch(0.56 0.245 27.325)`.
   - I recomputed it with per-channel clipping and got 4.82:1 on `#F7F5F0` and 5.01:1 for white on destructive. The report claims 4.59 and 5.01, so the paper figure differs but both are at or above 4.5.
   - `docs/design.md` §2.4 (table row) and §13 are updated with the "darkened to meet §11 AA on paper" note.

**Extra commit 51adb35 (gold keycap ledge literal)** — No breakage.
- `--keycap-ledge-gold: #705300` matches `--color-gold-700` (`src/app/globals.css:30`) in both light and dark.
- Line 242 still reads `var(--keycap-ledge-gold)`, so the keycap utility is unaffected.
- This fixes the Tailwind v4 tree-shaking issue, where `@theme` variables no utility uses are not emitted.

**New Breakage in the Fix Diff**

None Critical or Important. Minor:
- `src/components/ui/button.tsx`: `{...props}` is spread after `disabled` and `aria-busy`, so a caller passing `aria-busy` explicitly would override it. Harmless.
- `src/components/ui/button.tsx`: with `render` or asChild-style composition, the wrapped label span could interfere. Nothing in slice 1 uses that.

**Out-of-Scope Observations**

- `docs/design.md:66` still lists measured contrast values with the older destructive numbers. The destructive row itself is updated, so this is only a doc consistency point.
- Report concern 2, input border contrast at rest, remains an open design.md decision. The fix diff does not touch it.

**Verdict**

**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage.

### Spec Compliance

✅ Spec compliant for everything I could verify from the diff:
- Step 1: `signUpSchema` rejects `@child.jarso.invalid`, case-insensitively, at src/features/auth/schemas.ts:748-755. Tests are at schemas.test.ts:715-731.
- Routes and prefixes: `/parent`, `/child` and `/invite` were added to `protectedRoutePrefixes` (site.ts:548-553). The proxy matcher uses `prefix` or `prefix/`, so `/sign-in/child` and `/start` stay public. I checked src/proxy.ts:5-6.
- Dashboard role router (dashboard/page.tsx:90-93): `requireRole("parent")` sends a child to `/child` and a signed-out user to login. I confirmed this in dal.ts:36-41. A parent goes to `/parent` with a membership, otherwise to `/parent/setup`. The session read is inside Suspense.
- Actions (server/actions.ts):
  - All three use `createAction`.
  - `createFamilyAction` uses `assertRole("parent")` and `redirect(safeNextPath(next, routes.parent))` (:251-258).
  - `addChildAction` uses `assertRole` and `redirect` (:260-275).
  - `setChildPinAction` uses `assertParentOf` (:277-280). It has no refresh, which is fine because nothing on screen changes.
  - `redirect` survives `createAction` because lib/action.ts:65 calls `unstable_rethrow`.
- Setup form:
  - The currency default comes from `navigator.language` through `currencyForLocale`, and GBP is the fallback (currency.ts:1201-1210).
  - The timezone comes from `Intl` in a client component, read with `useSyncExternalStore`, so there is no hydration mismatch (create-family-form.tsx:916-925).
- Add child: nickname, PIN with `inputMode="numeric"`, and the consent label exactly "I am this child's parent or guardian" (add-child-form.tsx:798-820).
- Parent home:
  - Family code card titled "Give this code to your child" (family-code-card.tsx:1024).
  - Children list with the switcher and a Reset PIN sheet.
  - "Add a child" and "Accept an invitation" links.
- `ChildSwitcher` props are `{ children: ChildSummary[]; activeChildId?: string }` (child-switcher.tsx:849).
- Layouts: parent/layout.tsx is static. Every page's session read sits inside `<Suspense>`.
- Barrels: `@/features/family` exports the `"use server"` actions the same way `@/features/auth/index.ts` does, and no deep cross-module imports remain.

⚠️ Cannot verify from diff:
- **Consent stamping:** `addChildAction` validates `consent` but does not pass it on. `consent_at` and `consent_by` must be set inside `register_child`, which is Task 6 SQL. `consent_by` does appear in supabase/migrations/20261028182342_child_accounts.sql. The controller should confirm `register_child` stamps both fields.
- **Invite token loss:** adding `/invite` to protected prefixes in this task means a signed-out parent opening `/invite?token=…` reaches `/login?next=/invite` and loses the token, because proxy.ts:17 passes `pathname` only. Task 8 must fix this, either in the proxy or by keeping the search string.
- **Smoke test against the hosted project (Concern 1 in the report):** this is a process issue, not a code issue. The implementer drove sign-ups against the hosted Supabase project, not a local stack. This likely created one unconfirmed `smoke…@gmail.com` user and sent a confirmation email to a third-party gmail address. The user needs to delete that auth user, and future implementer briefs should forbid builds and smoke tests against `.env.local`.

### Strengths
- The authorization layering is correct.
  - The actions re-assert role and parentage before calling services that assert again.
  - `addChild` returns FORBIDDEN without a membership.
  - A CONFLICT from the service becomes a nickname field error with `cause` kept (actions.ts:1266-1271).
- `createFamilyAction` validates `next` with Zod and sanitises it with `safeNextPath`, so there is no open redirect.
- Browser values are read with `useSyncExternalStore` and a server snapshot, so there is no effect and no hydration mismatch. Submit stays disabled until the timezone is known.
- `currencyForLocale` uses `Intl.Locale().maximize()`, so `ja` maps to JPY. Bad input falls back safely, and the table test covers the edge cases (currency.test.ts).
- Design work:
  - `@/components/form` moves `Field` into a shared module, adds the error icon, and wires the helper text into `aria-describedby` (§6/§11). The docs barrel table was updated.
  - The `rise-in` utility follows §8 exactly, and reduced motion now zeroes `animation-delay` (globals.css:422), so nothing stays hidden during a delay.
  - The 36 px `sm` button appears only in the parent list. The switcher uses `aria-current` as the non-colour signal. Reset PIN has an sr-only "for {nickname}".

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None in code. The hosted-Supabase side effect above needs the user's action. It is a process finding, not a code defect.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **Possible redirect loop** at src/app/(app)/parent/page.tsx:207. When a membership exists but `getFamily()` returns null (RLS mismatch), the page redirects to `/parent/setup`. setup/page.tsx:337 sees the membership and redirects back to `/parent`, so the two pages loop. Fix: throw `AppError("NOT_FOUND"/"INTERNAL")` instead of redirecting.
2. **Empty state shows two actions** (parent/page.tsx:226-243). Its comment says "one action", and design.md §6 Empty state says "one action". It renders two: primary "Add a child" and outline "Accept an invitation". The brief requires both links on home, so either move "Accept an invitation" below the card or fix the comment.
3. **PIN fields may trigger password suggestions.** add-child-form.tsx:804 and set-pin-form.tsx:1105 use `autoComplete="new-password"` on PIN fields. Chrome may offer a generated strong password, or save the child PIN as the parent's site credential. Consider `autoComplete="off"` (or `one-time-code`).
4. **Consent error wording on a parent screen.** The error copy comes from the Task 6 schema (schemas.ts `consent`): "A parent needs to say yes first". The parent is the one reading it, so it reads oddly. Something like "Tick the box to confirm you're this child's parent or guardian" would fit §9's plain parent voice better.
5. **`cn` import is inconsistent.** child-switcher.tsx:843 imports `cn` from `"cn"`. Other feature code, including family-code-card.tsx:999, uses `@/lib`.
6. **`ProfileCard` is now unused.** The dashboard no longer renders it, and a grep finds no consumer outside `features/profiles/components`. Delete it or leave a note for later.
7. **Case-insensitive rejection is untested.** The sign-up schema test does not cover an uppercase `[email redacted]`, which the implementation handles.
8. **Title punctuation is inconsistent.** The H1 "Your family" (parent/page.tsx:303) has no full stop, while "Set up your family." and "Add a child." do.
9. **Copy-failure message is a toast** (family-code-card.tsx:1016). §6 says toasts are "never for errors that need action". The message is mild, so this is borderline.

Checks I ran:
- `cn` package: `"cn"` is a real dependency (package.json:32) and the ui primitives use it, so the import is not broken.
- Button sizes: `default` and `lg` are h-11 and `sm` is h-9 (button.tsx:33-35). The Select trigger is h-11 (select.tsx:43).
- Consent value: Base UI Checkbox submits the native "on", which matches the `consent` union in the schema.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** Every brief requirement and controller ruling is in the code, with correct authz layering, Suspense placement, safe `next` handling and design-token use. The remaining findings are minor polish. Separately, the user must clean up the hosted-project user created by the implementer's smoke test.

Status: DONE. A new parent who signs up from an invite link now keeps the token, so after sign-up or email confirmation they land back on `/invite?token=…`. `pnpm check` (95/95 tests) and `pnpm build` pass; the app was never run.

Commit: `22fbc65` fix(auth): carry next through sign-up

What changed:
- **Links:** "Sign up" on the sign-in form and "Sign in" on the sign-up form both carry `next`. A new helper, `withNext`, only adds it when `safeNextPath` accepts it.
- **Sign-up form:** `signUpSchema` accepts an optional `next`, `SignUpForm` sends it as a hidden field, and `/signup` reads `?next` inside `<Suspense>`, the same way `/login` does.
- **Sign-up action:** the confirmation email links to `/auth/callback?next=<safe next>`. When sign-up returns a session straight away it redirects to that path; without `next` it still goes to `/dashboard`.
- **`/auth/callback`:** already runs `safeNextPath` on `next`, so I left it alone.
- **`supabase/config.toml`:** added `http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/callback**` and `http://localhost:3000/auth/callback**` to the allowed redirect URLs. The existing entries are exact URLs, so a callback with `?next=` would not match.

Tests:
- New tests for `withNext` (safe `next` kept, unsafe ones dropped) and for `signUpSchema` keeping `next`. The `safeNextPath("/invite?token=…")` case was already added in `af64aa7`.
- New `src/features/auth/server/actions.test.ts` mocks the Supabase client and the redirect. It checks the email link, an unsafe `next` falling back to `/dashboard`, and both redirect cases.
- With the old action code, 3 of the 4 new action tests fail; with the fix all pass.

Decision for you: the hosted Supabase project needs the same `…/auth/callback**` redirect URL added in its dashboard, or confirmation links with `?next=` will not be accepted there. I did not touch the hosted project.

The fix round is appended to `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-28-slice-1-family-onboarding/task-8-report.md`, with the commands and output.
