### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant for everything that can be checked from the diff. Steps 1, 2, 4 and 5 are implemented. All four controller rulings are met:
  - **getClientIp:** `src/features/family/server/client-ip.ts:855-859` tries the first x-forwarded-for value, then x-real-ip, then returns "unknown". It is exported from the server barrel (`server/index.ts:868`).
  - **Login keeps the query string:** `src/proxy.ts:1126,1131` puts `pathname + search` into `next`. New tests in `src/features/auth/schemas.test.ts:434-437` show that `safeNextPath` still blocks `//evil.com/...` and `https://evil.com/...`.
  - **Consent is passed through:** the hardcoded `true` is gone. `repository.ts:907` sends `p_consent: args.consent`, carried via `service.ts:1069,1099` and `actions.ts:771`.
  - **One lookup per render:** `findInvitation` runs once per render at `invite/page.tsx:204`, after the membership check.
- Invariant 16 holds. `/start` collects only `nickname` and `parentEmail` (`start-onboarding-form.tsx:624-641`, `schemas.ts startChildOnboardingSchema`).
- The share card's copy matches the brief exactly (`invite-share-card.tsx:570-574`). It shows the code in large type (`:579-582`) and has a link copy button (`:587`).
- `/invite` covers every case:
  - Expired, used or unknown invitation: shows the brief's exact copy through `INVITATION_GONE` (`service.ts:1045`, `page.tsx:212-219`).
  - Parent with no family: sends them to `/parent/setup?next=<encoded /invite?token=…>` (`page.tsx:187-196`). I checked that the setup page and `CreateFamilyForm` honour `next` through `safeNextPath` (`parent/setup/page.tsx:21-23`, `create-family-form.tsx:44`).
  - No token: shows a code field (`:199`).
  - Accept form: nickname prefilled and editable, PIN, consent (`accept-invitation-form.tsx`). Success redirects to `/parent` (`actions.ts:796`).
- Two deviations, both acceptable:
  - The page lives at `src/app/(app)/invite/page.tsx`. The URL is the same and it picks up the app header with sign-out.
  - `?code=` works as well as `?token=`.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - Step 3, the manual end-to-end check, was not run. It is deferred to Task 10's e2e test, so the controller should make sure that test covers start → copy link → sign in → accept → child listed.
  - Whether email is sent only when RESEND_API_KEY is set lives in the existing `startChildOnboarding` service, which this diff does not change.

### Strengths

- The duplicate-nickname handling is factored into one helper, `nicknameConflict` (`actions.ts:756-764`), shared by `addChildAction` and `acceptInvitationAction`. No logic is duplicated.
- One message, `INVITATION_GONE`, covers unknown, expired and used invitations, so none can be told apart. The page and the action share it.
- The page checks for a family before calling `findInvitation`, so a parent without a family does not use up rate-limit budget. The service test asserts the same (`service.test.ts:1014-1019`).
- The service tests check the exact arguments passed to `registerChild`, including `consent` and `invitationId`. They also cover NOT_FOUND creating no auth user, and the auth user being removed when registration is rejected (`service.test.ts:949-1019`). These test real behaviour.
- Accessibility:
  - Focus moves to the share card title when it appears (`invite-share-card.tsx:552-554,569`).
  - Error notices use `role="alert"` plus an icon and words, so colour is never the only signal (`page.tsx:267-269`).
  - Tap targets are 44 px.
  - There is one keycap per child screen, and the parent screen uses the default primary button.
- The 8-character code is split 4+4 so it fits on narrow screens. Typed spaces are harmless because codes are normalised.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)

1. **A brand-new parent loses the invite token when they sign up.** This is a gap in the flow rather than in this diff.
   - `src/features/auth/components/sign-in-form.tsx:40` links to `routes.signup` without `next`.
   - `src/features/auth/server/actions.ts:19-26`: `signUp` ignores `next`, and `emailRedirectTo` is a bare `/auth/callback`. The callback (`src/app/auth/callback/route.ts:11`) supports `next`, but nothing passes it.
   - After sign-up the new parent ends up on dashboard → parent setup → `/parent`, without the invitation.
   - **Severity: Important, not Critical.** The child-started flow mainly serves parents who have no account yet, so this is the core first-milestone path. It can be recovered: the email tells the parent to "sign in or create a parent account, then open this link" (`service.ts:121-122`), the child's screen shows the code, and `/parent` has an "Accept an invitation" button that leads to the code form.
   - **Fix:** a small follow-up before the Task 10 e2e work.
     - Pass `next` from the login page to the sign-up link.
     - Add a hidden `next` to `SignUpForm` and to `signUpSchema`.
     - Set `emailRedirectTo` to `/auth/callback?next=<safeNextPath(next)>`.
     - When sign-up gives an immediate session, redirect to `safeNextPath(next)`.
     - The setup page already carries `next` onward.
   - It is outside Task 8's file list, so it should be its own task or a fix commit, not a reason to reject this task.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. `src/app/(app)/invite/page.tsx:169-183`: `token` and `code` from searchParams get only a trim, not Zod, which is a little short of CLAUDE.md rule 9. The values are only hashed, so the risk is low. A small `z.string().trim().max(64)` would bound the input.
2. `invite/page.tsx:244`: "The 8-letter code" is wrong, because the alphabet includes the digits 2-9. Use "8-character code".
3. Every reload of `/invite?token=…` costs one lookup out of 10 per hour per parent, and each submit costs another (`page.tsx:201-204` plus `service.ts acceptInvitation`). The implementer noted this. It is acceptable at this scale.
4. The `consent` transform (`schemas.ts:698-700`) can only ever produce `true`. The `boolean` type and the `consent: false` test (`service.test.ts:958-970`) only exercise the type path. This is fine under the ruling and noted for clarity.
5. The implementer admits there was no separate failing (RED) test run for the consent change. That is a process gap only; the tests now assert exact arguments.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** Task 8 meets the brief, the invariants and all four controller rulings, with good layering, accessibility and real-behaviour tests. The one significant gap is that sign-up drops `next`. It sits in the auth module outside this task's scope and can be recovered with the code, but it should be fixed as a follow-up before the e2e milestone.

**Finding Verdicts**

1. **New parent via invite link loses the token on sign-up** — ADDRESSED. Every hop in the chain now carries `next`:
   - Login "Sign up" link: `sign-in-form.tsx:92` uses `withNext(routes.signup, next)`.
   - `/signup` page: it reads `searchParams.next` inside `<Suspense>` and passes it to `SignUpForm`.
   - Form: `sign-up-form.tsx:126` renders a hidden `next` input. The "Sign in" link also carries `next` back.
   - Schema: `schemas.ts` `signUpSchema.next` is optional.
   - `emailRedirectTo`: `actions.ts` builds `/auth/callback?next=<target>` with `URL.searchParams.set`, where `target = safeNextPath(next)`.
   - Immediate-session redirect: `redirect(target)` replaces `redirect(routes.dashboard)`.
   - Callback: `src/app/auth/callback/route.ts` already runs `safeNextPath(searchParams.get("next"))` and resolves it against `origin` before redirecting.
   - Open redirect: closed at all three exits. The `emailRedirectTo` value, the immediate redirect and the callback all go through `safeNextPath`. `withNext` drops any `next` that `safeNextPath` would alter. `signUpSchema.next` is unvalidated, but the action sanitizes it before use.
   - Tests: `withNext` cases and the schema `next` case are in `schemas.test.ts`. The new `actions.test.ts` covers `emailRedirectTo` carrying a safe `next`, an unsafe `next` becoming `/dashboard`, a session redirecting to `next`, and the default `/dashboard`. The report shows RED then GREEN (3 of 4 failed on the old code) and `pnpm check` with 95 tests passing. I did not re-run them.

**New Breakage in the Fix Diff**

- Minor, `supabase/config.toml:~164`: the wildcard `…/auth/callback**` also matches paths like `/auth/callbackfoo`. The origin is fixed to localhost and 127.0.0.1, and the callback route does not serve those paths, so it is not exploitable. This is local config only.
- Minor, operational: the hosted Supabase project's Redirect URLs need an equivalent `<prod>/auth/callback**` entry. Without it, Supabase falls back to `site_url` and the token is dropped again in production. The implementer flagged this and did not touch the hosted project, so it is a deploy-time to-do.
- Minor, `sign-up-form.tsx:42`: `SignUpForm`'s `<Suspense fallback>` renders the form without `next` briefly. If a user submitted during that window, `next` would be lost. This is the same pattern as `/login` and is negligible.

**Out-of-Scope Observations**

- None.

**Verdict**

**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage. The only follow-up is adding the hosted Supabase redirect-URL wildcard before the invite flow ships to production.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every file the brief lists has its hunk, and every controller ruling is met:
  - **Form** (`child-sign-in-form.tsx`): the family code upper-cases as the child types (L302). The PIN field has `inputMode="numeric"` and `autoComplete="off"` (L329-330). There is exactly one keycap button, with `loading={pending}` (L338).
  - **Action** (`actions.ts:421-424`): `childSignInAction` uses `createAction` with the Zod schema, takes the IP from `getClientIp()`, and redirects to `routes.child`. The redirect survives because `createAction` calls `unstable_rethrow` (checked `src/lib/action.ts:65`).
  - **Copy**: the invalid and locked messages are exact (checked `service.ts:186,191,194`). There is no 15-minute promise.
  - **Login link**: `login/page.tsx:161-163` reads "Are you a kid? Sign in here".
  - **Signed-out child redirect**:
    - DAL: `dal.ts:536-539` sends a signed-out child to `routes.childSignIn`; parents still go to `requireUser(next)`.
    - Proxy: `proxy.ts:711-714` does the same and runs before the generic protected-prefix rule.
    - `/sign-in/child` and `/start` are not in `authRoutePrefixes` (checked `src/config/site.ts:30`).
  - **Child layout**: `child/layout.tsx` is static and renders `ChildNav`, whose only tab is Home → `/child` (`child-nav.tsx:254`).
  - **Child home**: `child/page.tsx:63-72` greets "Hi, <nickname>!" inside `<Suspense>` with `requireRole("child", routes.child)`.
  - **Table names**: they appear only in the repository (`repository.ts:597-605`).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff: the parent→`/parent` and child→`/child` cross-redirects come from the unchanged `homeOf`/`requireRole` (`dal.ts:533,551`). The logic is correct by reading, but only the "child passes" case has a test. Task 10's e2e should cover both cross-redirects and the proxy branch.

### Strengths

- **Security path is tight** (`service.ts:179-198`):
  - A session is minted only for `result.childId`, which comes back from `verify_child_pin`.
  - A wrong code, a wrong nickname and a wrong PIN all give one identical UNAUTHORIZED message.
  - The IP and family rate limits run before verification.
  - The IP comes from `getClientIp` (`actions.ts:422`).
- **DAL change is TDD'd and minimal.** `dal.test.ts:484-487` asserts that `requireUser` is not called for a child.
- **Form state handling is careful.** Code and nickname are controlled so they survive a failed try; the PIN clears. `FormError` is hidden while field errors show (L336), so there are no doubled messages.
- **Leftover `as Route` casts removed** (`dal.ts:533`, `start/page.tsx:233`) now that the pages exist.
- **Layering is followed:** page → service (`getChildNickname` with `assertRole`) → repository.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **Proxy branch untested** (`src/proxy.ts:711-714`). There is no unit test that a signed-out `/child` request redirects to `/sign-in/child` without `next`. It is optimistic only, since the DAL backs it, but a one-case proxy test or a Task 10 e2e assertion would lock the ruling in.
2. **Empty state has no action** (`src/app/(app)/child/page.tsx:75-85`). design.md:184 says an empty state is "mark, one sentence, one action". Here there are two sentences and no action. Leaving out the action is defensible because nothing is actionable yet, and it keeps the one-keycap rule trivially met. The second line could fold into the first to fit "one sentence".
3. **Double auth round on child home** (`src/features/family/server/service.ts:681-684`). `getChildNickname` runs `assertRole` right after the page's `requireRole`. `getRole` and `getMembership` are cached, so the extra cost is one `getUser`/`assertUser` call. It is harmless defence in depth; I note it only so nobody "optimises" it away.
4. **Locked state reveals an existing family-code + nickname pair** (`src/features/family/server/service.ts:190-191`). A non-existent pair can never be "locked", so the locked message tells an attacker the pair exists. This is inherent in the pre-existing `verify_child_pin` contract and is limited by the IP (20/15 min) and family (30/hr) limits. It is not introduced by this task; flagging it for the whole-branch review.
5. **Follow-ups from the report, confirmed and out of scope:**
   - Sign-out lands children on the parent `/login`.
   - The `AppHeader` home link goes to `/dashboard`, which costs a child one extra redirect.
   - A signed-in user who visits `/sign-in/child` is not bounced away; it stays public per the ruling.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** All brief steps and controller rulings are met. A child session is minted only after a successful PIN check, all wrong-detail errors share one message, and the IP-based rate limit uses `getClientIp`. The remaining items are test coverage and design polish, nothing that blocks.

USER DECISION (binding): the e2e setup may obtain local keys by running `supabase status -o json` (or `-o env`) PROGRAMMATICALLY inside the Playwright config/setup code and pass them to the test server's environment. Critical safety design (binding):

So the webServer command must receive NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:54321, NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY, SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY, NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL=<baseURL> via `webServer.env`, for both `pnpm build` and `pnpm start`. Playwright starts webServer BEFORE globalSetup, so compute the keys in playwright.config.ts itself (execSync of `pnpm exec supabase status -o json`, parse, keep in memory) — verify this ordering against the installed Playwright version. If local Supabase isn't running, the onboarding project must skip with a clear message (never fall back to .env.local).
- Hard guard: before any test runs, assert the URL used is 127.0.0.1/localhost; abort otherwise. Also have the e2e admin helpers (creating/confirming users if needed) use the local URL explicitly.
- Use a dedicated port for the e2e server (e.g. 3200) and reuseExistingServer: false for the onboarding project, so it never reuses a dev server running with hosted env. Port 3123 is held by an old process — don't touch it.
- Build output: building with local env overwrites .next; that's fine.

Status: DONE_WITH_CONCERNS

The e2e suite passes against local Supabase, but only after I fixed a real product bug it found: adding any child failed. That fix is a third commit you didn't ask for.

Commits:
- f20a727 fix(db): apply child role written after user insert
- 18ede73 test(e2e): cover both onboarding paths
- 3ab922b docs: document child sign-in and e2e setup

**The bug.** GoTrue's admin `createUser` inserts the user first and sets `jarso_role` in a later UPDATE, so the insert trigger gave every child a `parent` profile. I confirmed this from GoTrue's statement log (logging since turned off). `register_child` then raised JA409, and `nicknameConflict` showed it as "Another child in your family already uses this nickname". The pgTAP helper sets the role at insert time, so the DB tests never caught it. The fix is a new migration `20261029010105_child_role_from_app_metadata.sql`: an update trigger that sets the child role, but only on a profile that isn't in any family yet. It comes with a new pgTAP file (`child_role.test.sql`, 4 tests) and a line in `docs/database.md`. Regenerated types didn't change.

**Results (all after the final commits):**
- `pnpm check`: lint and typecheck clean, 98 unit tests pass.
- `pnpm build` with local env set inline: OK.
- `pnpm db:test`: 125 pass. DB lint is clean, and a fresh `db:reset` works.
- `pnpm test:e2e`: 7/7 pass (3 smoke + 4 onboarding). I ran it 4+ times after one DB reset with no rate-limit trouble.
- With local Supabase treated as not running, the 4 onboarding tests skip with a message and smoke still passes. In CI the run fails instead of skipping.
- No keys appeared in any output, and I never ran `supabase status` from my shell.

**How the safety design works:**
- **Key loading:** keys are read in `playwright.config.ts` itself, because Playwright 1.64.1 starts the webServer before globalSetup.
- **Server env:** the server runs on port 3200 with `reuseExistingServer: false`. `webServer.env` passes the local values for both build and start.
- **Blocking `.env.local`:** the server also gets `__NEXT_PROCESSED_ENV=true`, which stops Next.js applying `.env*` files at all. I proved this with a harmless dummy env file, since deleted.
- **Preflight check:** global setup creates a throwaway user on the local stack and signs it in through the app. If that fails, the run aborts.

**Rate limits:** no `db:reset` is needed between runs. Each run uses new `@example.com` parents and families plus a random `x-forwarded-for` (Next keeps a header the client sends). Per run that's 10 PIN attempts, 1 invite and 4 auth sign-ups/sign-ins. This is documented in `docs/testing.md`.

**CI:** the e2e job now installs the Supabase CLI pinned at 2.122.0 and runs `supabase start > /dev/null` before `pnpm test:e2e`. It hasn't run on GitHub yet.

**Concerns:**
1. The server logs many "Date.now() while prerendering" errors on /parent, /child, /dashboard, /invite and other pages. Pages render and tests pass. This was there before this task, but worth a look before deploy.
2. `nicknameConflict` turns every CONFLICT error into the nickname message, which is what hid this bug.
3. `__NEXT_PROCESSED_ENV` is an internal Next.js flag. If a future Next drops it, the Supabase settings are still overridden explicitly, but optional `.env.local` values like `RESEND_API_KEY` could load again. Test emails use `@example.com` as a backstop.

`docs/api.md` is unchanged because it doesn't list individual actions.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant for the e2e and docs scope, with one gap: brief Step 4 says to run the `security-auditor` and `code-reviewer` agents. The report says "No agents dispatched" (task-10-report.md:10). Step 5's `.claude-logs/` commit and PR are also not done (report:10). Both may be the controller's to own, but the brief assigns them to this task.
- Controller requirements, checked against the diff:
  - Local-only Supabase:
    - Keys are read in memory from `supabase status -o json`, with stderr dropped and errors swallowed (tests/e2e/local-supabase.ts:459-467).
    - `assertLocalUrl` hard-aborts on a non-local host, including a cached value (local-supabase.ts:449-454, 484, 494).
    - No keys are printed. CI discards `supabase start` stdout (ci.yml:86).
  - webServer gets local env for both build and start (playwright.config.ts:248-256, 275, 281).
  - Dedicated port 3200 with `reuseExistingServer: false` (config:245, 279).
  - Clean skip when local Supabase is down: spec.ts:519 and global-setup.ts:384-388. CI throws instead (global-setup.ts:385).
  - Parent-started path (spec:599-618) and child-started path (spec:620-655) are both covered.
  - Invite reuse shows the exact copy (spec:524-526, 653-654).
  - Loose code/nickname input: lower case, hyphen or space, surrounding spaces (spec:596-597, 614, 647).
  - Isolation (spec:660-665).
  - Child→/parent lands on /child (spec:676-678). Parent→/child lands on /parent (spec:668-669).
  - Signed-out /child goes to /sign-in/child (spec:681-683).
  - Lockout copy, and the correct PIN is still refused (spec:700-706).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: the CI e2e job has never run on GitHub (report:43). The controller should confirm the first CI run passes, including that `supabase start` in the e2e job does not need a separate `db reset`.
- `docs/api.md` is unchanged. That is acceptable: the brief says to modify it only "if actions are listed there", and the report says they aren't.

### Strengths

- The real root-cause bug was found and fixed instead of being papered over: GoTrue writes app_metadata in an UPDATE after the insert. The new pgTAP test reproduces GoTrue's actual insert-then-update order (child_role.test.sql:6-15), which the old `tests.create_user` helper hid.
- Migration security review (invariant 15). Named risk: can any user-writable path reach the role? Results of the checks:
  - Only `raw_app_meta_data` fires the trigger (migration:30). Users cannot write that column. Only the GoTrue admin/service role can.
  - The only app write site is `src/features/family/server/child-auth.ts:24`, a constant `{ jarso_role: "child" }`. I grepped `src` for `app_metadata|updateUserById|jarso_role`.
  - The trigger is one-way: it only ever sets `child` and can never promote anyone to parent.
  - It is a no-op for any profile with any `family_members` row, regardless of status, including `removed` (migration:20). So a parent in a family can't be demoted, and a member's role can't flip after joining.
  - The `WHEN old is distinct from 'child'` guard stops re-firing on GoTrue's later app_metadata updates, such as provider info on sign-in.
  - `profiles.role` remains non-updatable by `authenticated`: family_core.sql:36-37 grants update on `full_name`/`avatar_url` only.
  - The function has `security definer`, `search_path = ''`, and EXECUTE revoked.
  - Remaining exposure: a parent who has not yet created a family can be turned into a child, but only by service-role code that writes `jarso_role`. That is already trusted. No abuse path found.
- The global-setup preflight is a sound hard guard (global-setup.ts:400-427). It creates a user on LOCAL and proves the app server can sign it in. Against a hosted backend the sign-in just fails and creates nothing there.
- The exact pathname predicate `at()` avoids the `/sign-in/child` false match (spec:533-534).
- The PIN-field-clear wait makes each lockout attempt deterministic (spec:696).

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None blocking for this task.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **`playwright.config.ts:249`, `__NEXT_PROCESSED_ENV`.** This is an undocumented internal Next flag. The implementer verified it empirically (report:20) and documented the fallback.
   - If it breaks, the Supabase URL and keys stay safe: explicit process env wins, and the preflight catches any mismatch.
   - What would leak back in is optional `.env.local` keys, mainly `RESEND_API_KEY`. That means real sends to `@example.com` addresses.
   - An explicit empty override won't work, because `RESEND_API_KEY` is `z.string().min(1).optional()` (src/config/env.server.ts:8).
   - Acceptable for now. Suggested follow-up: a server-side `E2E`/`EMAIL_DISABLED` guard, or a test that asserts the flag still suppresses `.env.local` on Next upgrades.
2. **`onboarding.spec.ts:529, 543`, spoofed `x-forwarded-for`.** This is fine as a test-isolation trick, but it works only because `getClientIp` trusts the first client-supplied XFF hop (src/features/family/server/client-ip.ts:10) and `next start` passes it through. So the e2e suite in effect shows that `pin:ip` / `invite:ip` limits can be bypassed on any host that doesn't overwrite XFF.
   - In production on Vercel the platform sets XFF.
   - Per-family limits (30/h) and per-child lockout still bound brute force.
   - The test is also coupled to that weakness: if `getClientIp` is hardened, every run shares `127.0.0.1`, and the second run within 15 minutes hits `pin:ip` 20/15 min.
   - Not from this task. Worth a deploy-time note in docs/security.md or production-readiness.
3. **`local-supabase.ts:444, 499`.** The cache env var `JARSO_E2E_LOCAL_SUPABASE` holds the local secret key as JSON. Playwright merges `process.env` into `webServer`, so it is inherited by `pnpm build`, `pnpm start` and the Next server. These are local-only keys and nothing writes them to disk, so this is low risk. If the cache var is kept, it could be stripped from `serverEnv`.
4. **`child_role.test.sql:349-352`.** The "user metadata cannot set role" test updates `raw_user_meta_data`, which can never fire the trigger (wrong column). It shows only that `handle_new_user` ignores user metadata, not anything about the new trigger. Adding a case that updates `raw_app_meta_data` without `jarso_role` (or with `jarso_role` = 'parent') and asserts no change would test the WHEN clause.
5. **`spec.ts:693-702`, pre-existing semantics.** The 5th wrong PIN locks the account but returns `invalid` (child_accounts.sql:251-262), so the child sees "didn't match" and only the 6th try shows the locked copy. The test matches the current behaviour. The brief's "five wrong PINs → locked message" is a UX ambiguity for the product owner, not a defect in this task.
6. **Report concern 2.** `nicknameConflict` maps every CONFLICT, including JA409 "Child account is not available", to the nickname field error. That hid this bug. It is out of this diff, but worth a follow-up ticket.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The e2e covers every required journey and copy string against a verified-local stack, with no key exposure. The extra migration fixes a real role bug and keeps invariant 15: it is one-way, admin-only, and blocked for anyone in a family. What remains is the two process steps the brief assigns to this task (reviewer agents, logs/PR) and test-infrastructure fragility (`__NEXT_PROCESSED_ENV`, XFF trust) worth tracking.

The coordinator sent a message while you were working:
Fix round 1 for Tasks 10a/10b. One Important security finding (confirmed by the reviewer in node):

Open redirect via control characters in `next`. safeNextPath (src/features/auth/schemas.ts) only checks the `/`, `//`, `/\` prefixes. A percent-decoded `next` like "/\t/evil.com" (from `%2F%09%2Fevil.com`, also `%0A`, `%0D`) passes; then `new URL("/\t/evil.com", origin)` strips the tab → `//evil.com` → http://evil.com/. Reachable via /auth/confirm's callback-unwrap branch (attacker wraps their own valid token_hash → victim signed into attacker account then bounced off-site), and the same hole exists in /auth/callback and the signIn/signUp redirects.

Fix at the root in safeNextPath: reject any value containing ASCII control characters or whitespace (/[\x00-\x20\x7F]/ or equivalent), AND as defence in depth resolve with `new URL(value, "http://n.invalid")` and require origin === "http://n.invalid" and the result to start with a single "/" (return pathname+search+hash of the parsed URL). Keep existing accepted cases working (e.g. "/invite?token=abc", "/parent").
Tests: add to safeNextPath tests and to route.test.ts unsafe-next cases: "/\t/evil.com", "/\n/evil.com", "/\r/evil.com", " /evil", "/%09/evil.com" via the callback-unwrap path (`next=http://localhost:3000/auth/callback?next=%2F%09%2Fevil.com`), and the same for /auth/callback if it has a route test (add a minimal one if not). Show RED (tests fail on old code) then GREEN.
Run `pnpm check` and `pnpm test:e2e` (must stay green), commit `fix(auth): block control characters in next path`, append fix report with commands + output, reply with the short contract.

Address this before completing your current task.

Status: DONE. The open redirect through control characters in `next` is fixed in `safeNextPath`, so every path that uses it is covered.

Commit: d513fa3 fix(auth): block control characters in next path

- **Fix (`src/features/auth/schemas.ts`):** `safeNextPath` now rejects any value containing control characters or whitespace (`/[\x00-\x20\x7f]/`).
- **Defence in depth:** it also resolves the value against `http://n.invalid`, requires that origin and a single leading `/`, and returns the parsed path, query and hash. Values that worked before (`/invite?token=abc`, `/parent`) still work.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every item in 10a and 10b is present:
  - `connection()` is the first statement of `getCurrentUser`, with the comment (session.ts:517-519). The docs line is at docs/auth.md:70.
  - `/auth/confirm` route (route.ts:11-28) with a Zod `type` enum of email|signup (schemas.ts:444-448).
  - `routes.authConfirm` (site.ts:413).
  - Template plus config.toml entry (config.toml:546-548). `enable_confirmations` is untouched.
  - Login copy for `auth_confirm` and `auth_callback` (login/page.tsx:234-238).
  - Unit tests covering every case the brief lists (route.test.ts).
  - Hosted setup docs (auth.md:109-126) and an e2e test (email-confirm.spec.ts).
  - The sign-up action still sends `/auth/callback?next=…` (actions.ts:483-488), so Task 8's `next` is preserved through unwrapping.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - Whether GoTrue redirect-URL matching really needs `**` to match `?next=` (deploy.md:147). The controller should confirm this against GoTrue's allowlist glob semantics.
  - Hosted dashboard steps are manual and still outstanding (the report says so).

### Strengths
- The open-redirect design is sound for most bypass classes. I replicated `nextFromEmailLink` and ran it in node; these all fall back to `/dashboard`:
  - a foreign origin
  - a different port (`http://localhost:3001/x`)
  - userinfo pointing elsewhere (`http://localhost:[email redacted]/`)
  - `javascript:` (origin "null" is never allowed)
  - protocol-relative `//evil.com`
  - a top-level `/\evil.com`, or `/\t/evil.com` (the URL parser normalises these into a foreign host, which is rejected)
  - encoded slashes (`/%2F%2Fevil.com` stays same-origin and encoded)
- Token handling is clean. Logs carry only `{code, status}` or "malformed", never the token or email (route.ts:391-393).
- A malformed query never calls `verifyOtp` (tests at route.test.ts:341-350).
- Replay is covered end to end: reusing the link lands on `/login` with the message (email-confirm.spec.ts:624-628).
- Placement of `connection()` (10b) is safe:
  - Checked `getCurrentUser` callers: only session.ts and family/server/dal.ts, and no `"use cache"`, `unstable_cache`, `after()` or `dynamic = "error"` anywhere in src.
  - Checked the throw paths in node_modules/next/dist/server/request/connection.js. It throws only for the cache, unstable-cache, after and build-time scopes, none of which are used. In action and route-handler request scopes it is a no-op.
- Docs contain no Supabase project ref or key. `https://jarso.vercel.app` was already in deploy.md:145 and is a public app URL, not a secret.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **Open redirect through control characters in the unwrapped callback `next`** (src/features/auth/schemas.ts:464-468, used by src/app/auth/confirm/route.ts:389).
   - In the `/auth/callback` unwrap branch, `url.searchParams.get("next")` percent-decodes the value, and the result goes straight to `safeNextPath`. That function only checks the prefixes `/`, `//` and `/\`.
   - So `/%09/evil.com` decodes to `"/\t/evil.com"`, which passes the check. Then `new URL("/\t/evil.com", origin)` strips the tab, making `//evil.com`, which resolves to `http://evil.com/`.
   - Verified in node with the replicated function:
     - input: `next=http://localhost:3000/auth/callback?next=%2F%09%2Fevil.com`
     - `nextFromEmailLink` returns `"/\t/evil.com"`
     - the redirect goes to `http://evil.com/`
     - `%0A` behaves the same way
   - Exploit: an attacker signs up with their own email, takes their valid `token_hash`, and wraps it in this link. A victim who clicks it is signed into the attacker's account on the Jarso domain, then bounced to an attacker site. That is a credible phishing chain.
   - The same root cause already exists in `/auth/callback` (src/app/auth/callback/route.ts, `safeNextPath(searchParams.get("next"))` followed by `new URL(next, origin)`). The `signIn` redirect also uses `safeNextPath`.
   - Fix at the root, in `safeNextPath`. Reject any ASCII control character or whitespace (`/[\x00-\x1F\x7F]/`), or resolve with `new URL(next, "http://x")` and require the origin to stay `http://x`. Then add `callbackRedirect("/\t/evil.com")` and a `/%0A/` variant to the unsafe-next `it.each` in route.test.ts:330-339.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx:247-248: `?error` is looked up on a plain object without validation.
   - `?error=constructor` (or `toString`) returns a prototype function. It passes `FormError`'s `!message` check (src/components/form/field.tsx:54) and gets rendered as a React child (React warning, nothing shown).
   - CLAUDE.md rule 9 also asks for Zod on params.
   - Fix: parse with `z.enum(["auth_confirm","auth_callback"]).safeParse(error)`, or guard with `Object.hasOwn`.
2. src/app/auth/confirm/route.ts:388: `new URL(clientEnv.NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL).origin` is recomputed on every request. It could be a module constant. Trivial.
3. tests/e2e/email-confirm.spec.ts:591: skips when local Supabase is absent, including in CI. This matches onboarding.spec.ts:12, so it is consistent. The CI protection depends on global-setup failing first; check that the new `auth` project can't pass silently as skipped.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** Both tasks are implemented as briefed. 10b's `connection()` is correctly placed and safe for every current caller, and the confirm route's origin check holds against the listed bypass classes. However, the callback-unwrap path inherits `safeNextPath`'s control-character gap, which is a confirmed, reproducible open redirect after a successful `verifyOtp`. Fix it in `safeNextPath`, which also closes the same hole in `/auth/callback`, and add regression cases.

### Finding Verdicts

- **(Important, security) Open redirect via control characters in `safeNextPath`** — ADDRESSED.
  - `src/features/auth/schemas.ts:28` adds `UNSAFE_PATH_CHARS = /[\x00-\x20\x7f]/`. It rejects every C0 control, space and DEL before parsing (line 39).
  - Lines 41-44 resolve the path against the sentinel `http://n.invalid`. They require the origin to match and a single leading `/`, then return the normalised `pathname + search + hash`. This covers the root cause and also dot-segment collapses such as `/a/..//evil.com`.
  - `nextFromEmailLink` (`schemas.ts:74`) applies the same character check to the raw link value, then delegates to `safeNextPath`.
  - Every entry point goes through this function: `/auth/callback` (`route.ts:11`), `/auth/confirm`, including the callback-unwrap path (`route.ts:21`), the `signIn`/`signUp` redirects (`features/auth/server/actions.ts:16,20`), `family/server/actions.ts:35` and `parent/setup/page.tsx:22`.
  - Regression tests cover all three layers:
    - `schemas.test.ts`: tab, LF, CR, NUL, DEL, a leading space and an embedded space.
    - `confirm/route.test.ts`: the raw tab/LF/CR values plus the callback-wrapped `%09`, `%0A` and `%0D` forms.
    - `callback/route.test.ts`: a new file covering `%09`, `%0A`, `%0D` and `%2F%2F`.
  - The report shows RED output (13 failures on the old code) and GREEN output (60/60 focused, `pnpm check` 135/135, e2e 8/8).

Check I ran: I copied `safeNextPath` and `nextFromEmailLink` into node (`/tmp/snp/t.mjs`) and resolved each result against `http://localhost:3000`. All 37 direct cases and 9 email-link cases stayed same-origin.
- **Sent to `/dashboard`:**
  - `/\t/`, `/\n/`, `/\r/`, `/ /`, leading space
  - `/\\evil`, `/\\\t`, `/\t\\`, `\\/evil`, `//evil`
  - `http://evil`, `javascript:`
  - `/a/..//evil.com`, `/.//evil.com`, `/./\\evil`, `/..//evil`, `/%2e%2e//evil.com`
  - callback-wrapped `%2F%09%2F`, `%2F.%2F%2F`, `%2F%5C%09`
  - `http:\\\\evil.com/...`, `http://localhost:[email redacted]/`
- **Allowed but harmless:**
  - Encoded slashes stay encoded, e.g. `/%2F%2Fevil` → `/%2F%2Fevil` and `/%5C/evil.com`.
  - Unicode whitespace and look-alike characters (U+3000, U+00A0, U+2028, U+2029, U+0085, U+200B, U+FEFF, U+FF0F, U+2215) are percent-encoded into a same-origin path such as `/%E3%80%80/evil.com`.
  - `/?x=//evil` and `/#//evil` stay on the origin.
- **Legitimate values unchanged:** `/invite?token=abc` and `/parent/setup?next=%2Finvite%3Ftoken%3Dabc`.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff

- Minor, not blocking: `schemas.ts:44`. `safeNextPath` now returns the normalised path instead of the input. Because `withNext` (`schemas.ts:49`) compares against the raw input, it now drops `next` when the input isn't already canonical (for example `/a/b/../c` or a raw non-ASCII path) and links to the bare page. This is safe and only affects odd inputs; the app's own `next` values are already canonical.

Otherwise none.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

None.

### Verdict

**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage.

## Code review: slice 1, family and onboarding (4403fac..d513fa3, 36 commits)

I followed the code-reviewer.md template. I read the diff in passes:
- all four migrations in full
- the family server layer (repository, service, dal, actions, child-auth, client-ip, barrels)
- every new page and form
- the auth changes (schemas, session, `/auth/confirm`, proxy, login and signup)
- e2e setup, the CI diff, `config.toml` and the email template
- the plan, auth.md, and the rulings and minors in progress.md

I skipped the generated `database.types.ts` and the CSS and brand assets beyond the token check. I ran `pnpm check` locally: Biome is clean, tsc is clean, and Vitest passed 135 tests across 17 files. As instructed I did not run the pgTAP or e2e suites, start any server, or contact any Supabase project. I made no changes to the tree.

### Strengths
- **SQL ↔ service contract is clean.** `verify_child_pin` returns `(child_id, status)` and never raises, so the failed-attempt counter always commits (per the ruling). `repository.verifyChildPin` maps unexpected rows to INTERNAL instead of treating them as ok.
- **Lockout is solid.** Rows are serialised with `for update`, an unknown nickname costs the same bcrypt time, and the lockout escalates (`invitations.sql:277-345`).
- **Role is trustworthy.** It comes only from `raw_app_meta_data`. The follow-up trigger (`20261029010105`) fixes the GoTrue write-ordering bug, and it can only promote a profile that is in no family.
- **Atomic, consent-enforced registration.** `register_child` checks parent consent in the database, locks the invitation `FOR UPDATE` (so a double tap creates one child), and makes the credential insert idempotent. `addChild` deletes the auth user if registration fails (Review Focus 4).
- **Boundaries hold.** Server-only modules all start with `import "server-only"`. Client barrels export only `"use server"` actions, the same pattern as `@/features/auth`. There are no deep cross-module imports. The admin client is used only for service_role RPCs and child-auth.
- **Cache Components are correct.** Every session or searchParams read sits behind `<Suspense>`, layouts stay static, and `getCurrentUser` calls `connection()`.
- **Open-redirect hardening is thorough.** `safeNextPath` rejects control characters and checks the origin. `nextFromEmailLink` checks the origin before unwrapping `next`.
- **The e2e suite refuses non-local Supabase** (`assertLocalUrl` plus a preflight sign-in), and secrets stay off stdout.

### Issues

#### Critical
None.

#### Important (fix before merge)

1. **A parent's PIN reset does not end the child's existing sessions, and a child session can set itself a password that survives the reset.**
   - **Where:** `src/features/family/server/service.ts:200-203`; `set_child_pin` at `supabase/migrations/20261028182920_invitations.sql:230-270`; `supabase/config.toml:229` (`secure_password_change = false`).
   - **What happens:** `set_child_pin` only rewrites the bcrypt hash. Anyone holding a child session (an `@supabase/ssr` cookie that JS can read) can call GoTrue `PUT /auth/v1/user {password}` with no reauthentication. After that they can sign in with `<uuid>@child.jarso.invalid` plus that password, directly against GoTrue or via `/login`. That route bypasses the PIN, the lockout and the rate limits.
   - **Why it matters:** the sheet copy promises "The old PIN stops working straight away", and a parent would reasonably expect a reset to lock out whoever learned the PIN. Today it doesn't.
   - **Fix:** in `setChildPin`, after the RPC succeeds:
     - set a new random password with `admin.auth.admin.updateUserById(childId, { password: generateToken() })`;
     - revoke the child's sessions, either by deleting from `auth.sessions` for the child in a security-definer function or via admin signOut.
     
     Also consider blocking password sign-in for the child domain with a GoTrue password-verification hook.

2. **Rule 9 (Zod on every external input) is not met, and some inputs have no length limit.**
   - `src/app/(app)/invite/page.tsx:16-17,29-30` reads `token` and `code` from searchParams with no schema.
   - `childSignInSchema` (`src/features/family/schemas.ts:45-49`) has no `.max` on `familyCode`, `nickname` or `pin`. The client caps them at 16/20/6, but the server does not.
   - `acceptInvitationSchema.invite` (`schemas.ts:39`) is also unbounded.
   - **Why it matters:** the raw `familyCode` becomes the rate-limit key `pin:family:<code>`, stored in the `usage_counters` primary key. A key of a few KB exceeds the btree row limit, which surfaces as INTERNAL, and every request with a distinct code adds a row. This was deferred in Task 8, but it is a hard-rule violation.
   - **Fix:** add a small Zod schema for the invite searchParams, plus `.max(16)` / `.max(20)` / `.max(6)` on the sign-in fields and `.max(128)` on `invite`.

3. **The login page crashes on `?error=__proto__`.**
   - **Where:** `src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx:19,31`.
   - **What happens:** `linkErrors[error]` on an object literal returns `Object.prototype`, and `FormError` renders that object, so React throws and the login page fails to render.
   - **Why it matters:** anyone can send a crafted link that breaks login. This was deferred in Task 10ab, but it is a one-line fix.
   - **Fix:** use `Object.hasOwn(linkErrors, error)` or a `Map`. Do the same in `src/lib/db-error.ts:13`.

4. **PIN fields use `autoComplete="new-password"`.**
   - **Where:** `add-child-form.tsx:33`, `accept-invitation-form.tsx:43`, `set-pin-form.tsx:61`.
   - **What happens:** on the parent's device the browser offers to save the child's PIN as a site password (with the nickname as username) and may offer a generated "strong password" in a 4–6 digit field.
   - **Fix:** `autoComplete="off"`, as already done in `child-sign-in-form.tsx`. This was deferred in Task 7.

5. **Input borders fail WCAG 1.4.11, which design.md §11 requires.**
   - **Where:** `--input: #E6E2DA` (`src/app/globals.css:152`) on white cards is about 1.3:1. Non-text contrast needs 3:1, and this affects every form in the slice.
   - **Precedent:** Task 6a's ruling on `--destructive` already established that §11 wins.
   - **Fix:** change this one token and update design.md §13. If the designer must decide, record it as an explicit known issue rather than a silent deferral.

#### Minor (can follow)

6. **A double-tap or race on accept shows the wrong copy.** The losing request gets JA404 from `register_child`, which `db-error.ts:6` turns into "We couldn't find that." instead of `INVITATION_GONE` (Review Focus 3). In `acceptInvitation` (`service.ts:160-176`), map NOT_FOUND to `INVITATION_GONE`.
7. **`nicknameConflict` maps every CONFLICT to the nickname error** (`actions.ts:40-48`). The other JA409 branch ("Child account is not available") can't be reached with a freshly created child id, so this is cosmetic. Defer.
8. **Stale lockout copy.** The JA423 mapping (`db-error.ts:8`, "wait 15 minutes") is now dead code and contradicts the escalating lockout. Remove it or reword it.
9. **Data minimisation (invariant 16).**
   - The rate-limit key `invite:email:<plain email>` (`service.ts:109`) stores parent emails in `usage_counters`, which is never pruned.
   - `invitations.parent_email` is kept after acceptance or expiry.
   - Hash the email in the key, and plan a cleanup job (cron slice).
10. **`/invite` makes an uncached, rate-limited lookup on every page load or reload** (10 per hour per parent), and `acceptInvitation` repeats it. Acceptable for now.
11. **The add-child and accept forms reset after a failed submit** (`add-child-form.tsx:18-38`). React resets uncontrolled fields after the action returns, so a nickname clash makes the parent retype the nickname and PIN and tick consent again. `ChildSignInForm` already uses controlled fields to avoid this.
12. **Timezone check mismatch.** `createFamilySchema` checks the timezone with `Intl`, while the database checks `pg_timezone_names` (`family_core.sql:225`). If the database rejects it, the parent gets a generic VALIDATION error with no way to fix it, because the timezone is a hidden field taken from the device. Fall back to UTC, or attach a field error.
13. **Two harmless defaults.**
    - `findProfileRole` returns "parent" when the profile row is missing (`repository.ts:33-37`). The database functions re-check roles, so this is harmless. Defer, or throw instead.
    - The `/parent` ↔ `/parent/setup` redirect loop (`parent/page.tsx:21`) can't happen while membership and families use the same RLS predicate. Defer, or throw INTERNAL instead of redirecting.
14. **Orphan auth users.** If `createChildAuthUser` fails ambiguously (`child-auth.ts:17-32`), an auth user can be left behind. It can't sign in because it has no membership. Defer.
15. **`getClientIp` trusts `x-forwarded-for`** (`client-ip.ts:9-10`). This is fine on Vercel, which overwrites the header, but it should be documented in docs/security.md as triaged. The per-child lockout still limits brute force regardless.
16. **Confirm-link fallback.** If `emailRedirectTo` is not allowlisted, GoTrue falls back to the Site URL, and `nextFromEmailLink` sends the user to `/` instead of `/dashboard`. Treat a bare root as the fallback.

### Triage of deferred minors and the "final triage" list (progress.md)

**Must fix before merge:**
- T7 PIN `autoComplete` → Important #4
- T8 `/invite` searchParams not Zod-bounded → Important #2
- T10ab login `?error` prototype keys → Important #3
- T6a input-border contrast (WCAG 1.4.11) → Important #5, or record it as an explicit known issue

**Should fix (cheap), may follow:**
- T10 `getClientIp` XFF trust: add the docs/security.md note
- T5 timezone aliases → #12
- the JA423 copy → #8

**Defer:**
- T6 orphan auth user on an ambiguous failure
- T6 `findProfileRole` defaulting to parent
- T7 setup redirect loop
- T10 `nicknameConflict` mapping every CONFLICT
- T10 5th wrong PIN shows "didn't match" (product decision)
- T4 `find_invitation` granted to authenticated
- T2 missing `on delete` on FKs and the removed-member JA409 (no deletion or removal flow yet)
- T9 child sign-out lands on `/login`, the extra `/dashboard` hop, and signed-in users not bounced from `/sign-in/child` or `/start`
- T6a keycap shadow, badge read order and safe-area minors
- T3 window-rollover and anon-execute test gaps
- Task 0 doc wording

### Declined to judge
- A parent signed in on a shared device who uses `/sign-in/child` silently swaps to the child's session. The plan doesn't cover this, and it is reasonable on a family tablet.
- An 8-character invite code is stored as unsalted SHA-256. A DB leak would make codes guessable within 7 days. Outside the threat model of a 60-user pilot.
- Holding a family code lets someone throttle that family's sign-in (`pin:family` 30/h). Accepted by the ruling.
- I did not run the pgTAP or e2e suites (no servers or Supabase, per instructions). I relied on progress.md for their pass status.
- I did not review design fidelity screen by screen against design.md beyond tokens and copy.

### Recommendations
- Add a pgTAP or unit test that a PIN reset revokes the child's sessions once #1 is fixed.
- Add unit tests for `requireRole` / `assertParentOf` (deferred in T6) before slice 2 builds on the DAL.

### Assessment
**Ready to merge: With fixes**

The schema, RLS, SQL↔service contracts, DAL role flow and Cache Components usage are sound, and the cross-task integration holds together. Nothing is Critical. Merge after the five Important items, the PIN-reset session revocation (#1) above all, plus the three trivial hard-rule and hygiene fixes (#2–#4).

## Security audit: feat/family-onboarding (4403fac..d513fa3)

I found nothing Critical or High. The core authorization holds up:
- Every write runs through a security-definer function with `search_path=''`.
- Tables are revoke-by-default, and RLS is on everywhere.
- `child_credentials`, `usage_counters` and `invitations` have no policies.
- `verify_child_pin`, `hit_rate_limit` and `create_invitation` can only be run by the service role.
- The role comes only from `raw_app_meta_data`, and the app-metadata trigger can't demote anyone who is already in a family (invariant 15 holds).
- `register_child`, `set_child_pin` and `create_family` check the caller's role and family inside the database.
- `safeNextPath` and `nextFromEmailLink` correctly block `//`, `/\`, control characters and other origins.
- I found no credential-shaped strings in the diff, docs or the new `.claude-logs`. The only emails are test fixtures (`[email redacted]`, `[email redacted]`).

### Medium

**M1. A child account can get a password that a PIN reset doesn't remove**
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/config.toml:229` (`secure_password_change = false`), `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/server/child-auth.ts:19-25`, and the `set_child_pin` function in `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261028182920_invitations.sql:230-269`.
- Risk: anyone holding a child's session (the child, or a sibling on a shared tablet) can call `supabase.auth.updateUser({ password })` with the publishable key. They can then sign in with `<uuid>@child.jarso.invalid` plus that password through GoTrue or `/login`. This skips the PIN lockout and survives a parent's PIN reset. A PIN reset also doesn't end existing child sessions.
- Fix:
  - Add a GoTrue Password Verification hook (or a Custom Access Token hook) that rejects password sign-in when `app_metadata.jarso_role = 'child'`.
  - In `setChildPin`, after the RPC succeeds and parent authorization has passed, call admin `updateUserById(childId, { password: generateToken() })` and `admin.signOut` for the child, so a reset logs the child out everywhere.
  - Also reject the child email domain in `signInSchema`.

**M2. Anyone who knows the family code can keep every child in that family from signing in**
- This is the known lockout item, with my severity judgement.
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/server/service.ts:184` (`pin:family:${familyCode}`, 30 per hour) and `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261028182920_invitations.sql:313-337` (the escalating 24-hour lock).
- Risk:
  - Anyone with the family code (it is shared between the children, so a classmate could have it) can send 30 requests an hour and block every child in the family, indefinitely.
  - Adding a nickname, they need only 5 requests a day to hold that child in a 24-hour lock.
  - A PIN reset clears the lock, but the attacker can re-lock it right away, and the "locked" response confirms the nickname exists. For a children's app this is a ready-made bullying tool.
- Fix:
  - Key the family limiter as `(familyCode, ip)` rather than just the family code.
  - Count lock failures per `(child, ip)`, and keep only a much higher global cap per child.
  - Write an `audit_log` row (`child.locked`) and notify the parent when a lock happens.
  - Return the same "invalid" status while locked unless the request comes from an IP that has already signed in successfully.

**M3. Personal data is kept indefinitely, against invariant 16**
- Where:
  - `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261028182920_invitations.sql:27-40`: `parent_email` is kept after acceptance and on rows that are never accepted, and nothing ever sets the `'expired'` status.
  - `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/server/service.ts:108-109,183`: rate-limit keys store the parent email in plain text (`invite:email:<email>`) and the IP addresses of signed-out children (`pin:ip:`, `invite:ip:`) in `usage_counters`, which is never cleaned up.
- Risk: invariant 16 says to store only a nickname and the parent email until approval. In practice the parent email and children's IPs are kept forever, in rows that grow without limit.
- Fix:
  - In `register_child`, set `parent_email = ''` (or make the column nullable and set it to null) when an invitation is accepted.
  - Add a pg_cron retention job that deletes or anonymises invitations past `expires_at` and deletes `usage_counters` rows older than about 2 days.
  - Hash the identifying part of each limiter key, e.g. `sha256(pepper || email)` and the same for IPs, before calling `hit_rate_limit`.

**M4. The public `/start` form can be used to send email to any address**
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/server/service.ts:106-137`, with the schema at `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/schemas.ts:3-8,33-36`.
- Risk: anyone, without signing in, can make Jarso email any address, with 20 free-text characters (which could be a URL or a scam line) in both the subject and body. The per-email limit (3 a day) can be bypassed with `+tag` or dotted variants of the address, and the IP limit falls to rotating IPs. This damages the Resend sending domain's reputation and gives phishers a trusted sender.
- Fix:
  - Limit nicknames to letters, numbers, spaces and common name punctuation, e.g. `^[\p{L}\p{N} _.'-]+$`, which blocks `/` and `:`. Apply the same rule in `create_invitation`/`register_child`.
  - Don't put the nickname in the subject line (use a fixed one).
  - Normalise the email before using it as a limiter key (strip the `+tag`, lowercase).
  - Add a global daily cap, e.g. `invite:global` at about 200 a day, and consider a CAPTCHA (Turnstile) on `/start`.

**M5. The secret-scanning safety nets don't cover the new Resend key**
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/scripts/check-secrets.sh:6`, `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.claude/hooks/extract-log.py:22-52` and `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.gitleaks.toml`.
- Risk: `RESEND_API_KEY` is a new kind of credential. A bare `re_…` key in a prompt, log or commit is only caught if it appears on a `KEY=value` line. Nothing else redacts or blocks it in this public repo, and I couldn't confirm that gitleaks' default rules include Resend.
- Fix: add `re_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}` (a Resend key pattern) to all three: the pre-commit regex, `REDACTIONS`, and a `[[rules]]` entry in `.gitleaks.toml`.

### Low

**L1. `find_invitation` can be called directly by any signed-in user, skipping the service rate limit** (known item)
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261028182920_invitations.sql:97-109,362`.
- Risk: any signed-in user, children included since it isn't limited to parents, can call it directly and skip the 10-per-hour limit. Codes are 8 characters from a 32-letter alphabet (about 40 bits), stored as unsalted SHA-256, so brute-forcing one is impractical at PostgREST speeds. A hit would only reveal a nickname and invitation id, letting an attacker take over or block an invitation.
- Fix: revoke from `authenticated` and run the lookup through the admin client after `assertRole("parent")`. `findInvitation` already enforces the limit.

**L2. `getClientIp` trusts the first `x-forwarded-for` hop** (known item)
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/server/client-ip.ts:10-11`.
- On Vercel this is fine, because Vercel overwrites the header. If the app is ever put behind Cloudflare or another proxy, or self-hosted, the per-IP limits disappear. Every request without the header also shares one `"unknown"` bucket.
- Fix: prefer `x-vercel-forwarded-for` or `x-real-ip` (set by the platform). Document that this code depends on Vercel.

**L3. The sign-in fields have no maximum length**
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/schemas.ts:45-49`.
- `familyCode`, `nickname` and `pin` only check a minimum. Because `familyCode` goes into a rate-limit key, an input of several KB exceeds Postgres's btree row limit and returns an INTERNAL error; it also bloats the counters table. Large PINs are also sent to bcrypt for no reason.
- Fix: cap them at `familyCode.max(12)`, `nickname.max(20)`, and `pin.regex(/^\d{4,6}$/)` (or `max(6)`).

**L4. Children can write a real name into their own profile**
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261028181734_family_core.sql:36-37`.
- `grant update (full_name, avatar_url)` applies to children too. A child can set a real name through PostgREST, and the family-visibility policy then shows it to the rest of the family. This goes against data minimisation.
- Fix: add `role = 'parent'` (or `not exists child membership`) to the profiles update policy's check, or leave child `full_name` for the parent to manage.

**L5. Whoever holds an invitation code gets guardian rights**
- Where: the `acceptInvitation` function at `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/server/service.ts:160-176`.
- Acceptance isn't tied to the stored `parent_email`, so whoever holds the code (for example, someone who saw the child's screen or got a forwarded screenshot) is recorded as the consenting guardian. The invitation is then used up and can't be accepted again.
- Fix: on `/invite`, show the masked address the invitation was sent to (`p***@domain`) so the person accepting can see a mismatch. Optionally, require the accepting parent's verified email to match when the email path was used.

**L6. The invite token travels through redirect URLs**
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/proxy.ts` (where `next` includes the `search` query string) and `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/auth/server/actions.ts`, where `emailRedirectTo` includes `/invite?token=…`.
- The raw token ends up in the `/login?next=` URL, in GoTrue's stored redirect, and in the confirmation email. The token is single-use and expires in 7 days, and `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` stops it leaking to other sites, so this is low risk.
- Fix (optional): exchange the token for a short-lived httpOnly cookie on the first visit to `/invite`, and remove it from the URL.

**L7. The e2e tests depend on Next's internal `__NEXT_PROCESSED_ENV` flag** (known item)
- Where: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/playwright.config.ts`.
- If Next changes how this flag works, `.env.local` (pointing at the hosted project) could be loaded during e2e runs. This is mitigated: `assertLocalUrl` and the local-user sign-in check in `global-setup.ts` stop the run before any test writes data.
- Fix: also refuse to run if any server env value isn't local, or run the e2e server with a temporary working directory that has no `.env*` files. Otherwise this is informational.

### Checked and found sound
- The anti-enumeration timing in `verify_child_pin` and the row lock that serialises concurrent attempts.
- The `register_child` invitation branch: the row is locked with `FOR UPDATE`, single use is enforced, and the update happens in the same transaction.
- `addChild` deletes the orphaned auth user if registration fails.
- `mintChildSession` sends no email and only runs after an `ok` result.
- CI throws away the output of `supabase start`, and `local-supabase.ts` never prints its keys.
- `sendEmail` never logs the recipient or the message body.
- The child-facing wording is suitable for 11–14 year olds.

Grants: revoked from public, anon and authenticated; granted only to service_role (migration grants block at the end). Its only caller is `setChildPin`, after `assertParentOf`. pgTAP covers 42501 for authenticated, the refresh token cascade, and that other users' sessions are untouched.
  - Parent login: `signInSchema.email = parentEmail`, which refuses `@child.jarso.invalid` case-insensitively (auth/schemas.ts). Tests cover mixed case.
  - `config.toml`: `secure_password_change = true`. The hosted step is in docs/auth.md.
- **B: Zod bounds.** ADDRESSED.
  - `childSignInSchema`: familyCode `.max(16)` before trim, nickname max 20, pin `^\d{4,6}$`.
  - `inviteValue` max 128 is used by `acceptInvitationSchema`.
  - `inviteParamsSchema` (preprocess first value, `.catch(undefined)`) is used in invite/page.tsx `InviteContent`.

  - Tests cover `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString`, `hasOwnProperty` and an array value.

  - globals.css: light `oklch(0.64 0.012 85)`, dark `oklch(0.565 0.046 220)`. design.md §2 and §13 are updated.

  - `child_pin_attempts` has RLS on, no policies, and `revoke all … from anon, authenticated` (migration L37-58). pgTAP shows a parent gets 42501 on both read and delete.
  - `verify_child_pin` has the new 4-argument signature. It has no `raise` anywhere. The dummy `crypt(gen_salt('bf',10))` still runs for an unknown family or nickname. The `child_credentials … for update` row lock still serialises attempts across all sources.
  - Per-source lock: 5 failures → 15 min × 2^(n-1), capped at 24 h.
  - Cross-source cap: 100 failures freeze the child (`frozen_at`). `set_child_pin` and `register_child` clear all attempt rows and the cap.

  - Zod uses `^[\p{L}\p{Nd} _.'-]+$` after NFC. SQL uses `[[:alpha:][:digit:] _.''-]` in both `register_child` and `create_invitation`. pgTAP shows `Zoë O'Neil-2` is accepted, so Postgres `[[:alpha:]]` is Unicode-aware locally.

  - The only update policy (from create_profiles.sql) is dropped and replaced with `using/with check (id = auth.uid() and role = 'parent')`.
  - The column grant is still `update(full_name, avatar_url)`, so `role` itself can't be changed by clients, and `role` is set only from app_metadata.
  - pgTAP shows a child's update changes 0 rows.
- **J6: `find_invitation` no longer executable by authenticated.** ADDRESSED. It was already revoked from public and anon (invitations migration L355-358). The new migration revokes it from authenticated and grants it to service_role. `findInvitation` calls it through the admin client only after `assertRole("parent")` and the per-user limit. Covered by pgTAP (42501) and a service test ("refuses non-parents before any lookup").
- **J7: `x-forwarded-for` trust documented.** ADDRESSED in docs/security.md under "Client IP".

### Open question: does rotating the child's password sign out the child's other devices?

**Yes, it does.** I read upstream supabase/auth master (fetched from GitHub; this is not in node_modules):
- `internal/api/admin.go` `adminUserUpdate` calls `user.UpdatePassword(tx, nil)` whenever a password is supplied.
- `internal/models/user.go` `UpdatePassword` with `sessionID == nil` runs `Logout(tx, u.ID)`, which deletes all of the user's sessions. It also runs `ClearAllOneTimeTokensForUser`.

So in this branch:
- Every PIN sign-in on device B kills device A's refresh token. A is signed out once its access token expires (at most 1 h).
- In `setChildPin`, the rotation alone already ends every session. `revoke_user_sessions` is redundant there; it is harmless as a belt-and-braces step.
- The e2e revocation test therefore cannot tell which of the two did the revoking. pgTAP covers the RPC by itself.
- docs/auth.md says GoTrue "may" end other sessions. It should say "will".

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff

- **Important: one child can't stay signed in on two devices.** service.ts `childSignIn` calls `rotateChildPassword` before mint. Given the GoTrue behaviour above, a child who uses a phone and a family tablet signs the other device out on every sign-in. The fix works as specified (ruling A2), but this cost wasn't visible when the ruling was made. The controller should choose one of:
  - accept the cost and fix the docs;
  - rotate only on PIN reset, and rely on the Password Verification hook plus `secure_password_change`;
  - re-rule.
- **Important: `invite:global` can be used to block /start for everyone.** `enforceLimits` records a hit on every limit even when another limit denies, and `hit_rate_limit` increments before it compares (child_accounts.sql L285-292). One client on one IP sending 200 POSTs to /start uses up `invite:global` for the day: its per-IP 5/h limit refuses those requests but they still count toward the global cap. No IP rotation is needed. Cheap fix: check `invite:global` only after the per-IP and per-email limits pass (sequential `enforceLimits`).
- **Minor: two simultaneous PIN sign-ins can fail one device.** Rotation clears all one-time tokens for the user (`ClearAllOneTimeTokensForUser`). If device B's rotation lands between device A's `generateLink` and its `verifyOtp`, A's magic-link token is wiped and A gets INTERNAL. Rare, and a retry fixes it.
- **Minor: password strength settings could break child sign-in.** `generateToken()` (64 lowercase hex characters) goes through GoTrue `checkPasswordStrength` on every child sign-in and PIN reset. If the hosted project turns on character-class requirements (uppercase or symbols), every child sign-in and PIN reset would fail with INTERNAL. Child creation already had this exposure; the fix makes it hot-path. Either note "keep password requirements at default" in docs/auth.md Hosted setup, or generate a mixed-class token.
- **Minor: the cross-source window is fixed, not sliding.** The 24 h window starts at the first failure, so near a window boundary up to about 198 guesses fit inside a short span. Acceptable at 4–6 digit PINs and pilot scale.
- **Minor: non-Latin names with combining marks are rejected.** `\p{L}` and `[[:alpha:]]` exclude combining marks (`\p{M}`), so names typed in Devanagari, Thai and similar scripts (for example "नेहा") fail validation even though they are real names. This follows from the brief's charset; flagging it for the product owner.
- **Minor: docs wording.** docs/auth.md and the child-auth.ts JSDoc say GoTrue "may" end sessions; per upstream it does.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

- `verify_child_pin` returns `locked` (and `frozen`) early without spending bcrypt time. This is the same pattern as before; the status already reveals that the child exists, so the timing adds nothing new.
- `getClientIp` falls back to `"unknown"`. Every client without the header would share one lockout source and limiter bucket. That can't happen on Vercel; it matters for self-hosting only, which docs/security.md now flags.
- Implementer concern 3 still stands: `revoke_user_sessions` needs `postgres` to have DELETE on `auth.sessions` on the hosted project. Confirm at the first `db push`. Because of the GoTrue behaviour above, `setChildPin` would still have revoked sessions before the RPC failed; the parent would just see INTERNAL after a PIN change that actually succeeded.

### Verdict

**Fix round:** All findings A–J addressed. Two Important items are new in the fix diff and need a controller decision before merge:
1. Rotating the password on every PIN sign-in signs the child's other devices out, which is now confirmed GoTrue behaviour (decide or re-rule A2, then fix the "may" in the docs).
2. One client can exhaust `invite:global` because denied requests still count; order the limit checks so the global cap is only hit after the per-IP and per-email limits pass.

Diff reviewed in passes (migration, then service/repository/schemas, then UI/CSS/scanners, then pgTAP/e2e/docs). The only computations I ran were the contrast-ratio checks; the GoTrue behaviour comes from the upstream source.

Keep the token/code paths working unchanged. The list must be request-time (inside Suspense; no caching of per-user data).
4. Layering: list_my_invitations called only from the family repository; service `listMyInvitations()` (assertRole parent) and an id-check used by the accept action; types in types.ts; components in src/features/family/components; barrels updated (client-safe vs server-only split).

Note: local Supabase auto-confirms emails (enable_confirmations=false), so email_confirmed_at is set — verify.

## Review: parent's pending-invitations list on /invite (a4e5a13..c5b2a61)

**Spec verdict: mostly met.** The database function, the id check, the server/client split, the UI and the tests all match the spec. The one gap is the "confirmed email" property: it is only as strong as the Supabase project's email-confirmation setting, and nothing in the repo enforces or documents that dependency. Family unit tests pass (`pnpm vitest run src/features/family`: 7 files, 90 tests). I did not run e2e or pgTAP.

### Strengths
- **`list_my_invitations()`** (`supabase/migrations/20261029025041_list_my_invitations.sql`):
  - It is `security definer`, `search_path=''`, and only `authenticated` can execute it (`public` and `anon` are revoked).
  - It keys on `auth.uid()` joined to `auth.users` and `profiles`, so the caller cannot supply any input.
  - It checks `email_confirmed_at`, `role='parent'`, pending status and expiry, and compares emails with `lower()` on both sides. It never returns the email, and it caps at 50 rows.
  - The partial index expression matches the query.
- **Role can't be faked.** `profiles.role` comes from `raw_app_meta_data` only (family_core.sql:15-37), and users can't update the role column. A child, or a user who sets metadata at sign-up, cannot become a parent.
- **No TOCTOU problem.** `register_child` locks the invitation `for update` and re-checks pending and unexpired. Between the id check and the insert, an invitation can only become accepted or expired, never re-addressed. The `NOT_FOUND` from that race is mapped to `INVITATION_GONE`, and the orphan child auth user is deleted (unit test included).
- **Same error everywhere.** Foreign, used and non-UUID ids all give `INVITATION_GONE`, including the Zod uuid message (`schemas.ts:495`). `?id=` is parsed with Zod and dropped if it isn't a UUID. Token and code paths are unchanged, and the page takes token, then code, then id.
- **Layering is correct.** Route → service → repository; the RPC call lives only in `repository.ts`; server-only exports go through `server/index.ts`; the list is read per request inside the existing Suspense with no caching.
- **UI follows `docs/design.md`.**
  - Empty state is the mark, one sentence and one action (Refresh).
  - Buttons are `h-11` (44px).
  - Refresh uses `loading` with `useTransition`.
  - Screen readers hear "Accept Ava" and "Refresh invitations".
  - Timestamps use `<time dateTime>`, and the section has `aria-labelledby`.
- **pgTAP covers the main cases:**
  - anon gets 42501
  - case-insensitive match, newest first
  - expired, accepted and another parent's invitations excluded
  - unconfirmed email and child role see nothing
  - a session with no user id sees nothing
  - the parent still can't read the `invitations` table directly
  - 50-row cap

### Issues

**Critical:** none.

**Important**
1. **The confirmed-email check does nothing when Supabase auto-confirms, and the hosted setting it relies on is not documented.**
   - `supabase/config.toml:227` has `enable_confirmations = false`. With that, sign-up sets `email_confirmed_at` straight away.
   - So on any stack configured like this, someone can sign up as a parent with an unregistered address that isn't theirs (e.g. `[email redacted]`). They then see that address's pending invitations (child nicknames) and can accept them into their own family.
   - With auto-confirm, an email change via `updateUser` may also apply immediately. I believe that is how GoTrue behaves but did not verify it. If so, the same thing works through `updateUser({ email })`.
   - The e2e test only passes because of this: see the comment at `tests/e2e/onboarding.spec.ts:48`, where the parent sees the list right after sign-up without confirming.
   - **Fix:** in the `docs/auth.md` hosted checklist (around lines 54-69), add a required item: "Authentication → Providers → Email → Confirm email: **on** (`list_my_invitations` depends on it)", and keep "Secure email change" on as well. Mention the dependency in the `docs/database.md:40` entry.
   - Optionally, set `enable_confirmations = true` locally and have the e2e confirm through the admin API, as `email-confirm.spec.ts` already does, so the property is tested end to end.

**Minor**
2. **pgTAP doesn't assert the function's properties** (`supabase/tests/database/list_my_invitations.test.sql`). Add a check like `select is((select prosecdef and proconfig @> '{search_path=""}' from pg_proc where proname='list_my_invitations'), true, …)`, plus a `function_privs_are` / `has_function_privilege` check that `authenticated` can execute it. A future `create or replace` that drops these would otherwise go unnoticed.
3. **The e2e only checks the id path on the page, not in the action** (`tests/e2e/onboarding.spec.ts:1209-1217`). Rejecting a foreign id in `acceptListedInvitationAction` is covered only by a mocked unit test (`service.test.ts:816`). That is acceptable, but the e2e should also check that Otto's invitation is still pending and acceptable by code after Dana's session tried it.
4. **Invitations past the 50-row cap can't be accepted by id** (`service.ts` `findMyInvitation`). The id is checked against the capped list, so an older invitation beyond 50 shows "Gone". This doesn't matter in practice; noting it so the cap isn't raised or removed without thinking about it.
5. **`acceptListedInvitation` calls `assertRole("parent")` twice** (once in `requireFamilyId`, again inside `listMyInvitations`). `getRole` is cached, so the cost is negligible. It could pass the client and skip the second check.
6. **Accept button style in list rows** (`invitation-list.tsx:336-341`). `docs/design.md:175` says list buttons are `ghost`; this uses the primary style. Primary is defensible because it's the only action in the row, but it's a deviation from the doc.
7. **No rate limit on the list or accept-by-id paths.** I think this is fine: both need a signed-in, confirmed parent, the query is cheap and indexed, and the id check can't be used to discover other people's invitations. If anything, add `/invite` refreshes to the existing per-parent limit later.

**Already in main, not from this diff, for awareness:** `register_child(…, p_invitation_id)` is callable by any `authenticated` user and does not tie the invitation to the caller's email. That is by design, because the token and code paths accept invitations sent to any address. So "id must be in the caller's own list" is enforced in the service layer. A direct RPC call would also need an unlinked child-role profile id plus an unguessable invitation UUID, so the risk is low.

### Verdict
**Needs fixes:** one Important item. Fix #1 (at least the docs/hosted-config requirement); the Minor items are optional. The code itself is sound and could be approved once #1 is addressed.

Key files:
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261029025041_list_my_invitations.sql
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/config.toml (line 227)
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/docs/auth.md (hosted checklist, lines 54-69)
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/src/features/family/server/service.ts
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/tests/database/list_my_invitations.test.sql
- /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/tests/e2e/onboarding.spec.ts
